<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the equivariant cohomological Hikita conjecture for arbitrary finite quivers, including loops and multiple arrows. For any dimension and framing vectors, any commuting flavor torus, and a stability character whose semistable locus is stable and has free gauge action, the equivariant cohomology of the Nakajima variety is canonically isomorphic to the coordinate ring of the scheme-theoretic fixed locus of the stability cocharacter on the flavor-deformed Coulomb branch. This is an isomorphism of graded algebras over the common coefficient ring, retaining nilpotents and including the empty case.

- **claim-equivariant-hikita** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every datum <a href="#eq:gauge-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gauge-data">[eq:gauge-data]</a>, every commuting flavor torus $F$, and every regular stability character $\nu$, both coefficient maps are surjective and have the same kernel. They therefore induce a canonical isomorphism of graded $R$-algebras $$H_F^*(X;\mathbb C)\ \cong\ \mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}].$$ The gradings are ordinary cohomological degree and BFN homological degree, respectively. The assertion includes the empty case, whose ring is zero.
  - Explanation: The specified coefficient maps present the same graded algebra, including its nilpotents and the zero ring for an empty quotient.
- **claim-flavor-normalization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-trivial-summands)): For the purposes of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, one may assume that the flavor action assigns a character to each arrow summand and acts diagonally on each framing multiplicity space. Adding or removing a $G$-trivial representation with any flavor action preserves both coefficient-algebra quotients and all stability conditions.
  - Explanation: Diagonalizing commuting flavor actions and adjusting gauge-trivial summands preserves the coefficient quotients and stability conditions.
- **claim-minuscule-abelianization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-minuscule)): There is an injective coefficient-preserving algebra map from $A_F$ into $$\bigl(\operatorname{Frac}(P_F)[X_*(T)]\bigr)^{\mathcal W},\qquad e^\lambda e^\gamma=e^{\lambda+\gamma}.$$ Besides $R$, the algebra is generated by the dressed classes associated with all nonzero positive and negative minuscule coweights. For $p\in P_F^{\mathcal W_\lambda}$, the class has expression $$\label{eq:minuscule} \sum_{\lambda'\in\mathcal W\lambda} e^{\lambda'}p_{\lambda'} \frac{\prod_{\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(N)} \beta^{(-\langle\beta,\lambda'\rangle)_+}} {\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}_T(T_{t^{\lambda'}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^\lambda)}.$$ Each distinct translate occurs once, and the dressing is translated with the coweight. Denote this class by $M_{\lambda,p}$. Weights $\beta$ include their flavor components, but pairings use only the gauge components; factors with exponent zero are omitted. The class has charge $[\lambda]$. On charge zero, the BFN grading is twice the ordinary polynomial degree of the rational coefficients in this description.
  - Explanation: Abelianization embeds the Coulomb algebra into Weyl-invariant rational Laurent expressions. Dressed positive and negative minuscule classes generate it over the coefficient ring, with their topological charges and grading retained.
- **claim-flavor-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-flavor-base-change)): Let $F\to F'$ be a homomorphism of algebraic tori, and suppose the action on $N$ is obtained by restriction of an $F'$-action commuting with $G$. Then the natural maps are isomorphisms $$\begin{aligned} A_{F'}\otimes_{H_{F'}^*(\mathrm{pt})}H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})&\ \cong A_F,\\ B_{F'}\otimes_{H_{F'}^*(\mathrm{pt})}H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})&\ \cong B_F. \end{aligned}$$ They respect the coefficient maps, both gradings, and convolution.
  - Explanation: Restricting an extended commuting flavor action gives the stated change of coefficients for both the Coulomb algebra and its full-torus fixed quotient.
- **claim-local-regular-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-stability)): Let $\theta$ be a rational character parameter for which every semistable point of $\mu^{-1}(0)$ is stable and has trivial stabilizer. There is a neighborhood $U$ of $\theta$ in $X^*(G)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R$ such that the stable and semistable loci are unchanged for every rational $\theta'\in U$. One may choose such a $\theta'$ outside all proper hyperplanes $\theta'\cdot\mathbf b=0$ with $0<\mathbf b\leq\mathbf v$, and then clear its denominators by a positive integer. Empty loci are included.
  - Explanation: A regular rational character can be perturbed without changing the entire stable or semistable locus, and the perturbation can avoid the specified proper dimension hyperplanes.
- **claim-neutral-correspondence-factorization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization)): Let $u\in R$ be homogeneous of cohomological degree $d$, and suppose that in $A_F$ it has an expression $$\label{eq:neutral-word-relation} u=\sum_w M_{\lambda_{w,1},p_{w,1}}\cdots M_{\lambda_{w,k_w},p_{w,k_w}}, \qquad k_w\geq1, \qquad \sum_j[\lambda_{w,j}]=0\in\pi_1(G).$$ Every factor is a nontrivial dressed positive or negative minuscule generator of Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>, and every word is homogeneous of degree $d$. Coefficients in $R$ may be included in the dressings. Choose an ordering of the factors of each word. There are $Z$ as above bounding their final lattices and a common truncation <a href="#eq:finite-lattice-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-lattice-space">[eq:finite-lattice-space]</a> such that $$\label{eq:finite-relation-factorization} u\rho_Z=\sum_w\Psi_w \quad\text{in}\quad \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^d_{\mathsf K,L} (\mathcal F_Z,\mathcal F_{\mathrm{pt}}).$$ Each $\Psi_w$ factors through a cohomological shift of $\mathcal F_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_{w,1}}}$. Only the endpoints are required to lie in $Z$ or in the neutral component; all intermediate lattices are included in the common truncation.
  - Explanation: A homogeneous neutral convolution relation becomes a finite correspondence identity. Each summand factors through its chosen initial minuscule modification, and the truncation includes intermediate lattices outside the neutral component.
- **claim-stable-pair-surjection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-kernel-inclusion)): For every datum and regular stability in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the coefficient map induces a graded $R$-algebra surjection $$\label{eq:stable-pair-surjection} B_F\twoheadrightarrow H_F^*(X).$$
  - Explanation: Relations in the full-torus coefficient quotient vanish under the Higgs coefficient map, producing a graded surjection compatible with the coefficient ring.
- **claim-stable-section-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stable-section-degree)): Let $\mathcal P$ be a principal $G$-bundle on $\mathbb P^1$, equipped with a frame at infinity, and let $\sigma$ be a global section of $\mathcal P\times_G(T^*N)$. Suppose that its value $\sigma_\infty$ in this frame is $\nu$-stable and has trivial stabilizer. Then $\deg\mathcal L_\nu\geq0$. If $\deg\mathcal L_\nu=0$, the pair $(\mathcal P,\sigma)$ has a unique trivialization, agreeing with the given frame at infinity, in which $\sigma$ is the constant section $\sigma_\infty$.* *In particular, suppose that $\mathcal P$ is specified by a lattice $b\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G$, with its usual trivialization on $\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0\}$. In the degree-zero case its topological class is zero and $$\label{eq:based-loop-trivialization} b=hG(\mathcal O),\qquad h\in G(\mathbb C[t^{-1}]),\qquad h(\infty)=1.$$ In the off-zero frame, the section is $h\sigma_\infty$.
  - Explanation: A global cotangent section with a free stable value at infinity has nonnegative character-line degree. Equality forces a unique framed trivialization and a constant section; the lattice formulation then uses a based polynomial loop.
- **claim-stable-test-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stable-test-support)): The support of $\Phi_{W_B}\mathbb C_{M_B}$ is contained in the closed locus $\Sigma_B$ defined by $$\label{eq:stable-critical-equations} n\in s+L_-, \qquad p+y\in gN^*(\mathcal O)/t^lN^*(\mathcal O), \qquad b=gG(\mathcal O).$$ Here $n\in E_b$ is already part of the definition of $M_B$. If $B$ lies in a nonzero topological component whose character-line degree is nonpositive, then $\Sigma_B$ is empty and the test in <a href="#eq:stable-test-proper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-test-proper">[eq:stable-test-proper]</a> is zero.
  - Explanation: Fiber derivatives bound vanishing-cycle support by the extension equations for both members of the stable pair. A nonzero topological component of nonpositive character-line degree has no such support.
- **claim-neutral-quadratic-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic-test)): Assume $D\neq\varnothing$. The locus $\Sigma_Z$ is a closed graph $\Gamma\simeq U\times D$ in $M_Z$. There are compatible $\mathsf K$-equivariant identifications $$\label{eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles} \Phi_{W_Z}\mathbb C_{M_Z} \simeq \iota_{\Gamma,*}\mathbb C_\Gamma[-2r], \qquad \Phi_{W_{\mathrm{pt}}}\mathbb C_{M_{\mathrm{pt}}} \simeq \iota_{D,*}\mathbb C_D[-2r].$$ Under these identifications, the test of $\rho_Z$ induces ordinary restriction $$\label{eq:quadratic-restriction} H_{\mathsf K}^*(U\times D)\longrightarrow H_{\mathsf K}^*(D)$$ with the same uniform shift on source and target. This map is split surjective and $R$-linear.
  - Explanation: On the neutral family, the critical locus is a closed graph and the function has a globally split quadratic normal form. The tested identity-lattice morphism becomes ordinary equivariant restriction with an identical shift on both sides and a coefficient-linear splitting.
- **claim-loop-laurent-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-laurent-model)): Under the abelianization embedding of Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>, the no-flavor Coulomb algebra is $$\label{eq:laurent-model} A_0=\left(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb Z^\Omega}P r_\lambda\right)^{\mathcal W}.$$ The identification preserves the coefficient ring $R_0=P^{\mathcal W}$ and the topological grading.
  - Explanation: With the selected loops and trivial flavor, cancellation of minuscule tangent denominators gives a polynomial-coefficient Laurent model, preserving coefficient classes and topological charge.
- **claim-multipartition-filtration-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-filtration-bound)): The quotient $B_0$ for the full topological torus satisfies the coefficientwise graded bound $$\label{eq:filtration-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}(B_0;q)\ \leq \sum_{m\in\mathcal M(\mathbf v)} q^{C(\mathbf v)-D_m+\mathbf w\cdot(\mathbf v-\mathbf l)}h_m(q).$$ Every exponent displayed in the sum is a nonnegative integer.
  - Explanation: The finite multipartition filtration bounds each graded dimension by shifted invariant cut quotients. The displayed shifts are nonnegative integers; this comparison does not provide an algebra presentation of the filtered quotient.
- **claim-finite-cut-quotients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient)): For every finite array $m$, the algebra $P_m/J_m$ is finite dimensional. Consequently $h_m(q)$ is a polynomial with nonnegative integer coefficients.
  - Explanation: The cut ideal has no common zero away from the origin, or is the unit ideal. The quotient and its finite-group invariants are therefore finite dimensional and have polynomial Hilbert series.
- **claim-cut-recurrence** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cut-recurrence)): For every finite array $m$, $$\label{eq:cut-recurrence} \frac{1}{(q)_m} =\sum_{k+m'=m} q^{w(k)+b(k,m')}\frac{h_{m'}(q)}{(q)_k}.$$
  - Explanation: Stratifying by unreachable slots yields a convolution recurrence for invariant cut-quotient Hilbert series. Contributions are indexed by type counts, and taking invariants introduces no additional subset multiplicity.
- **claim-cut-generating-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio)): In the formal power series ring $\mathbb C(q)[[z_i:i\in Q_0]]$, $$\label{eq:cut-generating-ratio} \sum_m z^{\\|m\\|}q^{-D_m-w(m)}h_m(q) = \frac{\displaystyle\sum_m z^{\\|m\\|}q^{-D_m-w(m)}/(q)_m} {\displaystyle\sum_m z^{\\|m\\|}q^{-D_m}/(q)_m}.$$
  - Explanation: The recurrence becomes a formal generating-series ratio after applying the quadratic cross-term identity. Division is valid because the denominator has constant coefficient one.
- **claim-hausel-cut-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hausel-identification)): For a quiver with a loop at every vertex of positive dimension, $$\label{eq:hausel-cut-bound} P_{X_{\det}(v,w)}(q) =\sum_{\\|m\\|=v} q^{C(v)-D_m+w\cdot(v-l)}h_m(q).$$
  - Explanation: At determinant stability with the loop hypothesis, the cut-quotient sum equals the ordinary Poincaré polynomial. The proof uses a topological moment-level comparison and the grading conventions in Hausel’s formula.
- **claim-loop-coefficient-isomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-loop-isomorphism)): Suppose that the quiver has a loop at every vertex of positive dimension. At determinant stability, the coefficient map induces a canonical isomorphism of graded $R_0$-algebras $$B_0\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^*(X_{\det}(v,w);\mathbb C).$$
  - Explanation: The stable-pair surjection and matching graded dimension bounds force the original coefficient map to be an isomorphism at determinant stability, retaining nilpotents and the empty case.
- **claim-regular-betti-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-betti-independence)): Fix a quiver and dimension and framing vectors. The ordinary Betti numbers of $X_\theta=\mu^{-1}(0)/\!/_{\theta}G$ are independent of the regular character $\theta$, where regular means that every semistable point is stable and the action on the semistable locus is free. Empty quotients are included.
  - Explanation: All regular characters for fixed quiver, dimension, and framing data give the same ordinary Betti numbers. The comparison includes simultaneous emptiness and uses topological rotation rather than a holomorphic identification.
- **claim-flavor-isomorphism-lift** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-flavor-lift)): For a fixed regular stability, suppose that the coefficient map induces $B_0\simeq H^*(X)$. Then, for every commuting flavor torus $F$, the map of Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-inclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-inclusion">8</a> is an isomorphism $$B_F\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_F^*(X)$$ of graded $R$-algebras.
  - Explanation: A coefficient-compatible ordinary isomorphism lifts to any commuting flavor torus. Freeness of equivariant cohomology and the bounded-below coefficient grading force the kernel of the lifted surjection to vanish.
- **claim-loop-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-loop-removal)): For every finite quiver and every dimension and framing vector, at the determinant stability used in Corollary <a href="#cor:loop-isomorphism" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:loop-isomorphism">20</a>, the coefficient map induces an isomorphism of graded $R_0$-algebras $$B_0\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^*(X).$$
  - Explanation: Adding weighted loops permits a local comparison with the original theory. Equality of the localized coefficient ideals descends through homogeneity to the original graded ideals, removing the loop hypothesis at determinant stability.
- **claim-full-torus-isomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-full-torus)): For every datum in the statement of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the coefficient map induces a canonical isomorphism of graded $R$-algebras $$B_F\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_F^*(X).$$
  - Explanation: Loop removal, regular-stability Betti independence, and flavor lifting identify the full topological-torus fixed quotient with equivariant Higgs cohomology under the original coefficient map.
- **claim-polynomial-monopole-maps** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-monopole-maps)): For every unframed quiver $\Gamma$ and dimension vector $\mathbf d$, the two halves above admit $S$-linear algebra maps to an algebra of difference operators defined without inverting rotation or arrow parameters. They send $e_{\mathbf n,g}$ and $f_{\mathbf n,g}$, for $\mathbf n\leq\mathbf d$, to nonzero constant multiples of the positive and negative fundamental operators, respectively; they send these elements to zero when $\mathbf n\not\leq\mathbf d$. After setting $\hbar=0$, $w_{ia}=x_{ia}$, and $u_a=-f_a$, where $f_a$ is the prescribed flavor weight of arrow $a$, their images lie in $A_{\Gamma,\mathbf d,F}$. Dimension-vector degree becomes positive, respectively negative, topological charge. These maps preserve finite polynomial-coefficient identities under this specialization.
  - Explanation: The polynomial shuffle halves map to difference operators without parameter inversions. Their distinguished images specialize to Coulomb classes, so finite polynomial identities remain valid after setting rotation to zero and assigning the given arrow flavors.
- **claim-indecomposable-charge-generation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-root-charge-generation)): Let $\Gamma$ be an unframed quiver with dimension vector $\mathbf d$, and let a flavor torus act by a character on each arrow. Write $A_{\Gamma,\mathbf d,F}$ for its commutative Coulomb algebra and $R_{\Gamma,\mathbf d,F}$ for its coefficient algebra. For $\epsilon\in\{+1,-1\}$, let $M_{\mathbf b,p}^{\epsilon}$ be a dressed minuscule class whose coweight has $b_i$ entries equal to $\epsilon$ at vertex $i$ and all other entries zero, where $0<\mathbf b\leq\mathbf d$. There is a finite expression $$\label{eq:root-charge-words} M_{\mathbf b,p}^{\epsilon} =\sum_r c_r a_{r1}\cdots a_{rk_r}, \qquad c_r\in R_{\Gamma,\mathbf d,F},$$ in which every factor is topologically homogeneous and $$\deg_{\mathrm{top}}a_{rh}=\epsilon\boldsymbol\gamma_{rh}, \qquad \boldsymbol\gamma_{rh}\in\Delta_\Gamma, \qquad \sum_{h=1}^{k_r}\boldsymbol\gamma_{rh}=\mathbf b.$$ The factors need not themselves be minuscule classes.
  - Explanation: Each dressed minuscule class admits a finite coefficient-linear word expression with homogeneous charges supported on Kac indecomposables. General stabilizer-invariant dressings are allowed, and the factors need not be minuscule.
- **claim-indecomposable-moment-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-root-moment)): If $\boldsymbol\gamma\in\Delta_\Gamma$ and $\boldsymbol\theta\cdot\boldsymbol\gamma=0$, then $$\mu_{\mathbb C}^{-1}(0)\cap \mu_{\mathbb R}^{-1}(\tau_{\boldsymbol\theta})\ne\varnothing.$$ The scalar circle acting simultaneously on all nonzero vertex spaces fixes every point of this representation.
  - Explanation: An indecomposable charge orthogonal to the central character supports a point at the required real moment level and zero complex level. The simultaneous scalar circle acts trivially on this block.
- **claim-cocharacter-full-torus-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cocharacter-fixed)): Suppose that every $\nu$-semistable point of $\mu^{-1}(0)$ is stable and has trivial $G$-stabilizer. Then the natural quotient map $$A_F/\langle (A_F)_m:m\ne0\rangle \longrightarrow B_F,$$ where $m$ denotes $\nu$-weight, is an isomorphism. This is an equality of quotients of $A_F$, preserving the coefficient map and both gradings.
  - Explanation: Regularity forces all nontrivial minuscule classes to vanish in the cocharacter quotient. Complementary surviving classes would yield semistable moment blocks with a positive-dimensional effective stabilizer. The cocharacter and full-torus defining ideals therefore coincide.

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a principal $G$-bundle on $\\mathbb P^1$, equipped with a frame at infinity, and let $\\sigma$ be a global section of $\\mathcal P\\times_G(T^*N)$. If $\\deg\\mathcal L_\\nu=0$, the pair $(\\mathcal P,\\sigma)$ has a unique trivialization, agreeing with the given frame at infinity, in which $\\sigma$ is the constant section $\\sigma_\\infty$.*\n\n\n*In particular, suppose that $\\mathcal P$ is specified by a lattice $b\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}_G$, with its usual trivialization on $\\mathbb P^1\\setminus\\{0\\}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **quiver gauge datum** ([source](#eq:gauge-data)): Let $Q=(Q_0,Q_1)$ be a finite oriented quiver. Loops and multiple arrows are allowed. Fix complex vector spaces $V_i,W_i$ of dimensions $v_i,w_i$, and put $$\label{eq:gauge-data} G=\prod_{i\in Q_0}\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V_i),\qquad N=\bigoplus_{a:i\to j}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(V_i,V_j) \oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_0}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(V_i,W_i).$$
- **regular stability character** ([source](#eq:gauge-data)): Choose a character $\nu$ of $G$ for which the $\nu$-semistable locus in $\mu^{-1}(0)$ equals the stable locus and $G$ acts freely there. We call such a character *regular*, and set $$\label{eq:higgs-definition} D=\mu^{-1}(0)^{\nu\text{-stable}},\qquad X=D/G.$$
- **finite lattice family** ([source](#eq:finite-lattice-space)): Set $$\label{eq:finite-lattice-space} \begin{aligned} L&=t^{-l}N(\mathcal O)/t^lN(\mathcal O),& E_b&=gN(\mathcal O)/t^lN(\mathcal O),\\ S_B&=\{(b,n)\in B\times L:n\in E_b\},& f_B&:S_B\longrightarrow L. \end{aligned}$$
- **cut polynomials and cut ideal** ([source](#eq:cut-polynomial)): For every nonempty subset $S\subseteq\Omega_m^+$, put $$\label{eq:cut-polynomial} q_S= \prod_{d\in S}x_d^{w_{i(d)}} \prod_{\substack{d\in S\\ e\in\Omega_m^+\setminus S}} (x_d-x_e)^{b_{de}}.$$
- **invariant cut-quotient Hilbert series** ([source](#eq:cut-polynomial)): Let $J_m\subseteq P_m$ be the ideal generated by these cut polynomials, and set $$h_m(q)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}\bigl((P_m/J_m)^{\mathcal W_m};q\bigr).$$
- **reachable slot** ([source](#eq:cut-cohomology)): A slot is *reachable* if there is a directed path from the framing vertex to it on which every arrow coordinate is nonzero.
- **Kac-polynomial support** ([source](#pm-h-26)): For a finite quiver $\Gamma$, possibly with loops, write $$\Delta_\Gamma =\{\,0\ne\boldsymbol\gamma\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{\Gamma_0}: A_{\Gamma,\boldsymbol\gamma}(q)\ne0\,\}.$$
- **central character functional** ([source](#pm-h-27)): For a real vector $\boldsymbol\theta=(\theta_i)$, let $\tau_{\boldsymbol\theta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G_{\boldsymbol\gamma})^*$ be the central functional $$\tau_{\boldsymbol\theta}(\xi) =\sum_i\theta_i\operatorname{tr}(\xi_i).$$

| q-regular-quotient-dimension | complex dimension of a nonempty regular quotient | = | $2(\dim N-\dim G)$ | The dimensions of the gauge representation and gauge group. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-flavor-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-trivial-summands) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trivial-summands): Decompose gauge-isotypic summands, diagonalize their commuting torus multiplicities, and remove gauge-trivial cotangent factors by normalized Thom isomorphisms. -> Weighted arrow and framing form with unchanged coefficient quotients and regularity.
- **step-minuscule-presentation** [claim](#stmt-prop-minuscule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-minuscule): Apply classical BFN localization, the minuscule Euler formula, and quiver minuscule generation. -> Injective abelianization and coefficient generation after killing nonzero charges.
- **step-flavor-base-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-flavor-base-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flavor-base-change): Use even free equivariant homology on finite orbit filtrations, then quotient by the extended nonzero-charge ideal. -> Flavor change-of-coefficients isomorphisms compatible with convolution.
- **step-local-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-stability): Use the finite family of weight cones to preserve stable and unstable loci, then choose a rational perturbation avoiding the specified proper hyperplanes. -> Unchanged quotient and principal gauge bundle under a suitable generic perturbation.
- **step-finite-correspondences** [claim](#stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization): Build minuscule lattice chains, compute their Hom shifts, and prove the relation by faithful equivariant localization. -> Finite correspondence relation with initial-step factorizations and correct shifts.
- **step-stable-section-degree** [claim](#stmt-lem-stable-section-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stable-section-degree): Evaluate a nonvanishing semi-invariant on the section; in degree zero use the relatively ample GIT polarization and freeness. -> Nonnegative character degree and a unique trivialization in the equality case.
- **step-test-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-stable-test-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stable-test-support): Differentiate along section and dual fiber coordinates, then interpret the critical equations as global extension conditions. -> Support bound and vanishing on nonzero components of nonpositive character degree.
- **step-quadratic-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-test): Describe the neutral critical graph, show its critical value vanishes by the moment equation, and use the global split quadratic form. -> Uniformly shifted ordinary restriction with a coefficient-linear splitting.
- **step-kernel-inclusion** [claim](#stmt-prop-kernel-inclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-test-2): Order each neutral word with a nonpositive character-degree step first, annihilate that test, and use the split restriction to detect the coefficient class. -> Coefficient-compatible graded surjection from the full-torus quotient to Higgs cohomology.
- **step-laurent-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-laurent-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-laurent-model): Cancel loop and tangent Euler factors, then induct on squared coweight norm using compatible level decompositions and leading dressings. -> Invariant polynomial-coefficient Laurent model.
- **step-multipartition-filtration** [claim](#stmt-prop-filtration-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-filtration-bound): Filter by positive-slot length, eliminate zero-slot power sums by derivations, and annihilate averaged leading cut relations. -> Graded dimension bound by shifted invariant cut quotients.
- **step-cut-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient): Exclude any nonzero common coordinate value by the cut containing all slots with that value, then apply the Nullstellensatz. -> Finite-dimensional cut quotient and polynomial invariant Hilbert series.
- **step-cut-recurrence** [claim](#stmt-prop-cut-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cut-recurrence): Stratify by unreachable slots, prove normal Euler multiplication injective, and take exact permutation invariants in the Thom sequences. -> Invariant cut-quotient convolution recurrence.
- **step-generating-ratio** [claim](#stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio): Weight and sum the recurrence using the quadratic cross-term identity, then divide by the series with unit constant coefficient. -> Formal generating-series ratio.
- **step-hausel-identification** [claim](#stmt-prop-hausel-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hausel-identification): Rotate the flat hyperkähler moment levels, use affine Kempf–Ness, and match Hausel’s ordinary-cohomology formula with the cut ratio. -> Equality of the cut sum and the ordinary Poincaré polynomial.
- **step-loop-isomorphism** [claim](#stmt-cor-loop-isomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-loop-isomorphism): Compare every graded source and target dimension of the coefficient-compatible surjection. -> Coefficient-algebra isomorphism at determinant stability under the loop hypothesis.
- **step-betti-independence** [claim](#stmt-lem-betti-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-betti-independence): Perturb within regular neighborhoods, rotate to affine central fibers, use constructibility of compactly supported cohomology, and apply Poincaré duality. -> Ordinary Betti numbers independent of regular stability, including empty quotients.
- **step-flavor-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-flavor-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flavor-lift): Use Leray–Hirsch freeness, flavor base change, and a least-degree argument on the kernel. -> Isomorphism for arbitrary commuting flavor tori.
- **step-loop-removal** [claim](#stmt-prop-loop-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-loop-removal): Add loops with auxiliary flavor, compare invertible Coulomb Euler factors and the actual fixed stable prequotient, then descend localized homogeneous ideals. -> Ordinary coefficient isomorphism at determinant stability for arbitrary finite quivers.
- **step-full-torus-isomorphism** [claim](#stmt-thm-full-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-full-torus): Use Betti independence to make the ordinary surjection bijective for every regular character, then lift flavors. -> Canonical full-torus quotient isomorphism with equivariant Higgs cohomology.
- **step-polynomial-monopole-maps** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-monopole-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-monopole-maps): Take normalized formal leading terms of trigonometric operator maps, fix product signs, and show the polynomial halves land over the polynomial parameter ring before specialization. -> Polynomial-coefficient monopole identities that survive specialization.
- **step-root-charge-generation** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-charge-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-charge-generation): Use polynomial Hall generation in Kac-supported degrees, apply the monopole maps, and extend from selected-variable to all stabilizer-invariant dressings. -> Finite word decompositions in indecomposable topological charges.
- **step-root-moment-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-moment): Pass from Kac support to a complex indecomposable by constructibility, apply the trace-annihilator lifting argument, and rotate a closed-orbit moment representative. -> Nonempty required moment block fixed by the simultaneous scalar circle.
- **step-cocharacter-ideal-equality** [claim](#stmt-prop-cocharacter-fixed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cocharacter-fixed): Adjoin the framing vertex and ineffective scalar, pair surviving minuscule classes with complementary classes, and contradict freeness using decomposed moment blocks. -> Equality of the cocharacter and full-torus fixed defining ideals.
- **step-main-composition** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cocharacter-fixed-2): Compose the quotient-identifying isomorphism with the full-torus cohomology isomorphism and use coefficient surjectivity for uniqueness. -> Canonical equivariant cohomological Hikita isomorphism and equal coefficient kernels.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-finite-cut-quotient**: Every finite cut quotient is finite dimensional, and its invariant Hilbert series has nonnegative integer coefficients.
- **invariant-cut-recurrence**: The invariant cut Hilbert series satisfies the reachability recurrence, with contributions indexed by count vectors rather than individual subsets.
- **invariant-framing-only-cut-model**: When the remaining arrow multiplicities vanish and all positive slots have the same type, singleton cuts impose the framing power of each variable. The invariant quotient is represented by permutation orbits of monomials whose individual exponents are below that framing power.
- **invariant-empty-and-unit-cuts**: The empty array contributes the coefficient field. A nonempty array without framing has a unit full cut and hence the zero quotient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-general-isomorphism-certificate**: Treat agreement of finite cut Hilbert series as a certificate of the general equivariant Hikita isomorphism. -> The finite computation does not identify the coefficient-map kernels or the scheme-theoretic fixed ideals.
- **anti-use-reduced-fixed-locus**: Replace the fixed scheme by its reduced points before comparing it with cohomology. -> Removing nilpotent functions can discard the positive-degree information carried by the coordinate ring.
- **anti-use-subset-multiplicity**: Insert a binomial multiplicity for every selected subset into the invariant cut recurrence. -> Permutation invariants already combine subsets with the same count vector, so the extra multiplicity overcounts the contribution.
- **anti-use-filtration-as-presentation**: Use the filtration symbol quotients as an algebra presentation of the full topological fixed quotient. -> The filtration supplies graded vector-space bounds without determining multiplication between its stages.
- **anti-use-zero-weight-survival**: Assume that a monopole survives in the cocharacter quotient merely because its total weight vanishes. -> A neutral product can contain factors of nonzero weight, each killed by the defining ideal.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite cut recurrence gives an exact computation for a restricted constructive subcase: a common slot type with vanishing remaining arrow multiplicities. The independent reference enumerates permutation orbits in the quotient by the framing powers. This checks the recurrence and its empty and unit-cut cases; it does not certify the general geometric isomorphism.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer n and w.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 6 and 0 <= w <= 5; common slot type, no remaining arrows.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing Hilbert polynomial coefficients in ascending degree.
# TIME: Finite exact symbolic recurrence; no general complexity bound asserted.
# SPACE: Finite symbolic expressions and cached polynomials.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer and rational polynomial arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce a nonzero exit.
import json
import sys
from functools import lru_cache
import sympy as sp

q = sp.Symbol('q')

def validate(obj):
    if not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'n', 'w'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid input fields')
    n, w = obj['n'], obj['w']
    if type(n) is not int or type(w) is not int:
        raise ValueError('integer inputs required')
    if not (0 <= n <= 6 and 0 <= w <= 5):
        raise ValueError('outside checked domain')
    return n, w

@lru_cache(None)
def poch(n):
    return sp.prod(1 - q**j for j in range(1, n + 1))

@lru_cache(None)
def hilbert(n, w):
    if n == 0:
        return sp.Integer(1)
    value = 1 / poch(n)
    for k in range(1, n + 1):
        value -= q**(w*k) * hilbert(n-k, w) / poch(k)
    return sp.cancel(value)

def coefficients(n, w):
    if n == 0:
        return [1]
    if w == 0:
        return [0]
    polynomial = sp.Poly(hilbert(n, w), q)
    result = [int(polynomial.nth(d)) for d in range(int(polynomial.degree()) + 1)]
    if any(sp.Integer(result[d]) != polynomial.nth(d) for d in range(len(result))):
        raise ArithmeticError('noninteger coefficient')
    if any(c < 0 for c in result):
        raise ArithmeticError('negative coefficient')
    return result

n, w = validate(json.load(sys.stdin))
print(json.dumps({'coefficients': coefficients(n, w)}, separators=(',', ':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import itertools\nimport json\nimport sys\n\ndef validate(obj):\n    if not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'n', 'w'}:\n        raise ValueError('invalid input fields')\n    n, w = obj['n'], obj['w']\n    if type(n) is not int or type(w) is not int:\n        raise ValueError('integer inputs required')\n    if not (0 <= n <= 6 and 0 <= w <= 5):\n        raise ValueError('outside checked domain')\n    return n, w\n\ndef independent_coefficients(n, w):\n    counts = {}\n    for exponents in itertools.combinations_with_replacement(range(w), n):\n        degree = sum(exponents)\n        counts[degree] = counts.get(degree, 0) + 1\n    if not counts:\n        return [0]\n    return [counts.get(degree, 0) for degree in range(max(counts) + 1)]\n\ndef valid_output(obj):\n    return (isinstance(obj, dict) and set(obj) == {'coefficients'}\n            and isinstance(obj['coefficients'], list)\n            and bool(obj['coefficients'])\n            and all(type(c) is int for c in obj['coefficients']))\n\ntry:\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        n, w = validate(json.load(stream))\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        candidate = json.load(stream)\n    expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    truth = {'coefficients': independent_coefficients(n, w)}\n    accepted = (valid_output(candidate) and valid_output(expected)\n                and candidate == truth and expected == truth)\nexcept Exception:\n    accepted = False\nsys.exit(0 if accepted else 1)\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON object with exactly coefficients: the ascending integer coefficients of the invariant cut Hilbert polynomial. The zero polynomial is represented by [0], and trailing zero coefficients are omitted.",
    "reason": "The finite cut recurrence gives an exact computation for a restricted constructive subcase: a common slot type with vanishing remaining arrow multiplicities. The independent reference enumerates permutation orbits in the quotient by the framing powers. This checks the recurrence and its empty and unit-cut cases; it does not certify the general geometric isomorphism.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with integer n and w.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 6 and 0 <= w <= 5; common slot type, no remaining arrows.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing Hilbert polynomial coefficients in ascending degree.\n# TIME: Finite exact symbolic recurrence; no general complexity bound asserted.\n# SPACE: Finite symbolic expressions and cached polynomials.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer and rational polynomial arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce a nonzero exit.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nimport sympy as sp\n\nq = sp.Symbol('q')\n\ndef validate(obj):\n    if not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'n', 'w'}:\n        raise ValueError('invalid input fields')\n    n, w = obj['n'], obj['w']\n    if type(n) is not int or type(w) is not int:\n        raise ValueError('integer inputs required')\n    if not (0 <= n <= 6 and 0 <= w <= 5):\n        raise ValueError('outside checked domain')\n    return n, w\n\n@lru_cache(None)\ndef poch(n):\n    return sp.prod(1 - q**j for j in range(1, n + 1))\n\n@lru_cache(None)\ndef hilbert(n, w):\n    if n == 0:\n        return sp.Integer(1)\n    value = 1 / poch(n)\n    for k in range(1, n + 1):\n        value -= q**(w*k) * hilbert(n-k, w) / poch(k)\n    return sp.cancel(value)\n\ndef coefficients(n, w):\n    if n == 0:\n        return [1]\n    if w == 0:\n        return [0]\n    polynomial = sp.Poly(hilbert(n, w), q)\n    result = [int(polynomial.nth(d)) for d in range(int(polynomial.degree()) + 1)]\n    if any(sp.Integer(result[d]) != polynomial.nth(d) for d in range(len(result))):\n        raise ArithmeticError('noninteger coefficient')\n    if any(c < 0 for c in result):\n        raise ArithmeticError('negative coefficient')\n    return result\n\nn, w = validate(json.load(sys.stdin))\nprint(json.dumps({'coefficients': coefficients(n, w)}, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite cut-quotient recurrence supplies a concrete specification for exact symbolic computation of invariant Hilbert polynomials. This application concerns the counting subproblem; it does not implement the general cohomological isomorphism. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The moment-level comparison concerns topological identifications of hyperkähler quotients. It supplies no time evolution, collision model, or numerical integration construction for a game or simulation. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicitly defined cut products support a symbolic algebra utility that constructs the homogeneous ideal for a finite slot array. The invariant Hilbert polynomial can then be checked independently against the recurrence. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The recurrence is triangular over finite componentwise subarrays: the term with empty first component contains the desired invariant Hilbert polynomial, and every other term uses a smaller array. This yields a finite exact computation. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite arrays and slot permutations index polynomial quotients, but the paper establishes no storage representation, access operation, or data-structure performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The difference operators are algebraic maps over polynomial parameters. Their construction gives no accelerator architecture, arithmetic datapath, or parallel execution schedule. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The equality of coefficient-map kernels is a geometric algebra assertion under regular stability. It provides no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or hardness reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Polynomial Hall generation expresses monopole classes through homogeneous factors supported on indecomposable charges. It does not define training, statistical inference, or a learned representation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Reachability stratification computes cohomology contributions and a generating recurrence. Its use of graph cuts does not supply an objective function, feasible optimization problem, or optimality certificate. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Flavor base change preserves convolution and gradings when a torus action is restricted. This algebraic compatibility gives no communication protocol, replicated-state construction, or fault-tolerance result. |
| other (Exact computation of quiver-variety Betti polynomials) | True | strong | implementable | inferred | For the stated loop hypothesis and determinant stability, the paper gives a finite multipartition sum identifying the ordinary Poincaré polynomial. Its cut-polynomial terms are computable through the finite-array recurrence. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-quiver-gauge-scope** (formal_scope): The comparison applies to the gauge representation constructed from finite quiver data. It does not establish the corresponding assertion for arbitrary reductive gauge groups. Blocked use: ['Extend the theorem to an arbitrary reductive gauge group solely by analogy with the quiver construction.']
- **limit-regularity-and-freeness** (hypothesis): Every semistable point in the zero moment fiber must be stable and have trivial gauge stabilizer. The fixed-scheme comparison does not establish the result when this condition fails. Blocked use: ['Apply the coefficient-kernel equality to a singular semistable quotient with nontrivial stabilizers.', 'Replace freeness by an unchecked assumption of finite stabilizers.']
- **limit-commuting-flavor-action** (hypothesis): The flavor action is a linear torus action commuting with the gauge group. Flavor base change requires that the restricted action come from the specified extended commuting action. Blocked use: ['Apply the flavor comparison to a noncommuting action.', 'Use an unrelated flavor extension as a change-of-coefficients model.']
- **limit-complex-coefficients** (formal_scope): The cohomological comparison uses complex coefficients. The polynomial Hall input over rational parameters does not assert an integral cohomology comparison. Blocked use: ['Infer an integral or torsion-sensitive cohomology theorem from the stated complex-coefficient isomorphism.']
- **limit-scheme-structure** (formal_scope): The fixed quotient retains its scheme structure and nilpotents. Reduced points alone cannot supply the algebra compared with cohomology. Blocked use: ['Replace the fixed quotient by its radical quotient.', 'Identify the cohomology algebra from the set of closed fixed points.']
- **limit-cohomological-not-quantized** (formal_scope): The theorem concerns the commutative cohomological algebra. It does not prove the quantized refinement. Blocked use: ['Promote the comparison to a theorem about quantized Coulomb algebras or quantum cohomology.']
- **limit-grading-separation** (formal_scope): Topological charge, cocharacter weight, and cohomological degree have distinct roles. The Hilbert-series calculation does not use topological charge as its degree. Blocked use: ['Compute the graded dimension bound using cocharacter weights in place of half cohomological degrees.', 'Assume the entire Coulomb algebra has a nonnegative cohomological grading.']
- **limit-neutral-word-factorization** (hypothesis): The finite correspondence factorization requires homogeneous positive-length neutral words in nontrivial dressed minuscule generators. Intermediate lattices must remain inside the common truncation even when they are outside the neutral component. Blocked use: ['Apply the factorization to an arbitrary nonneutral relation.', 'Truncate only the endpoints and omit intermediate lattice constraints.', 'Discard correspondence shifts when testing a coefficient relation.']
- **limit-stable-test-component-condition** (hypothesis): The support-vanishing assertion requires both a nonzero topological component and nonpositive character-line degree. It cannot be applied indiscriminately to the neutral component. Blocked use: ['Annihilate the identity-lattice test merely because its character-line degree vanishes.']
- **limit-quadratic-test-nonempty-locus** (edge_case): The quadratic-test lemma assumes a nonempty stable locus. The proof of the coefficient surjection handles the empty locus separately. Blocked use: ['Invoke the nonempty critical-graph identification as evidence that the stable locus exists.']
- **limit-loop-dependent-counting** (hypothesis): The Laurent model and shifted cut sum use trivial flavor and a loop at every vertex of positive gauge dimension. The ordinary Betti identification uses determinant stability. The final general theorem removes these restrictions through separate arguments. Blocked use: ['Use the Laurent model directly with arbitrary flavor parameters.', 'Apply the stated cut sum directly to loopless data or an unspecified stability.']
- **limit-filtration-is-not-presentation** (formal_scope): The filtration gives graded vector-space surjections and dimension bounds. It does not supply an algebra presentation of the full topological-torus quotient. Blocked use: ['Transfer multiplication from the cut quotients to the full fixed quotient without a separate algebra argument.', 'Infer coefficient-kernel equality from the filtration bound alone.']
- **limit-empty-and-unframed-cases** (edge_case): The empty gauge array contributes the scalar ring, whereas an unframed supported component produces a unit cut and a zero quotient. An empty Higgs variety has the zero ring. Blocked use: ['Represent all degenerate inputs by the same scalar output.', 'Keep a constant class after a unit cut has killed the quotient.', 'Treat an empty quotient as evidence for a nonempty geometric instance.']
- **limit-formal-series-regime** (formal_scope): The generating-ratio identity is formal. Its division is justified by the denominator's unit constant coefficient, rather than by analytic convergence or arbitrary numerical evaluation. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the unsimplified rational expression at singular parameter values.', 'Infer convergence of the generating series from the formal identity.']
- **limit-loop-removal-descent** (hypothesis): Loop removal compares the actual fixed stable prequotient and localized coefficient maps, then descends equality through homogeneous ideals. A generic localized isomorphism alone does not establish the global graded comparison. Blocked use: ["Replace the prequotient fixed locus by the quotient's fixed-point set.", 'Conclude global ideal equality without checking homogeneity and coefficient compatibility.']
- **limit-rotation-is-topological** (formal_scope): Quaternionic rotation supplies identifications of underlying topological spaces. It does not assert a holomorphic identification in the original complex structure. Blocked use: ['Transfer algebraic or holomorphic structures solely through the moment-level rotation.']
- **limit-flavor-lift-premise** (hypothesis): The flavor lift requires an ordinary isomorphism induced by the coefficient map at a fixed regular stability. Its kernel argument uses target freeness and the bounded-below grading of the coefficient quotient. Blocked use: ['Lift an abstract vector-space identification as though it were a coefficient-compatible algebra isomorphism.', 'Apply the least-degree argument to the unrestricted Coulomb algebra.']
- **limit-polynomial-specialization** (formal_scope): The monopole maps preserve finite polynomial-parameter identities under the stated specialization. The argument does not assert injectivity or flatness after specialization. Blocked use: ['Specialize identities that require inverses of rotation or arrow parameters.', 'Infer a faithful specialized representation from the polynomial monopole maps.']
- **limit-zero-weight-does-not-imply-survival** (counterexample_scope): A minuscule class of zero total cocharacter weight need not survive the fixed quotient. It can be a product of factors killed by the nonzero-weight ideal. Blocked use: ['Replace the quotient by its weight-zero subspace.', 'Require nonvanishing on every bounded dimension vector as the definition of regularity.']
- **limit-existence-versus-finite-construction** (nonconstructive): The geometric existence arguments do not provide a finite procedure for constructing general moment-level points or coefficient-kernel isomorphisms. Finite exact cut computations certify only their explicitly restricted counting problem. Blocked use: ['Use a finite Hilbert-polynomial agreement as a certificate of the general geometric theorem.', 'Infer general resource bounds from finite dimensionality or existential Hall generation.']

          "exact_quote": "If there are no gauge slots, the unique empty array gives $A_0=B_0=\\mathbb C$ and equality in Equation <a href=\"#eq:filtration-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:filtration-bound\">[eq:filtration-bound]</a>. If $\\mathbf v\\ne0$ and $\\mathbf w=0$, the cut on all positive slots is $q_{\\Omega_m^+}=1$, so every summand on the right is zero. Correspondingly, $e^{\\mathbf 1}$ is an invertible monopole of nonzero topological degree, and $B_0=0$. The same observation applies to any unframed connected component. Full subsets, empty zero blocks, and rank-one gauge factors require no change in the proof.",
          "source_id": "PM-044.1"
        },

          "exact_quote": "For every unframed quiver $\\Gamma$ and dimension vector $\\mathbf d$, the two halves above admit $S$-linear algebra maps to an algebra of difference operators defined without inverting rotation or arrow parameters. They send $e_{\\mathbf n,g}$ and $f_{\\mathbf n,g}$, for $\\mathbf n\\leq\\mathbf d$, to nonzero constant multiples of the positive and negative fundamental operators, respectively; they send these elements to zero when $\\mathbf n\\not\\leq\\mathbf d$. After setting $\\hbar=0$, $w_{ia}=x_{ia}$, and $u_a=-f_a$, where $f_a$ is the prescribed flavor weight of arrow $a$, their images lie in $A_{\\Gamma,\\mathbf d,F}$.           "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be an unframed quiver with dimension vector $\\mathbf d$, and let a flavor torus act by a character on each arrow. For $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$, let $M_{\\mathbf b,p}^{\\epsilon}$ be a dressed minuscule class whose coweight has $b_i$ entries equal to $\\epsilon$ at vertex $i$ and all other entries zero, where $0<\\mathbf b\\leq\\mathbf d$. There is a finite expression $$\\label{eq:root-charge-words}\n M_{\\mathbf b,p}^{\\epsilon}\n   =\\sum_r c_r a_{r1}\\cdots a_{rk_r},\n \\qquad c_r\\in R_{\\Gamma,\\mathbf d,F},$$ in which every factor is topologically homogeneous and $$\\deg_{\\mathrm{top}}a_{rh}=\\epsilon\\boldsymbol\\gamma_{rh},\n \\qquad\n \\boldsymbol\\gamma_{rh}\\in\\Delta_\\Gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{h=1}^{k_r}\\boldsymbol\\gamma_{rh}=\\mathbf b.$$ The factors need not themselves be minuscule classes.",
          "source_id": "PM-044.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the equivariant cohomological Hikita conjecture for arbitrary finite quivers, including loops and multiple arrows. For any dimension and framing vectors, any commuting flavor torus, and a stability character whose semistable locus is stable and has free gauge action, the equivariant cohomology of the Nakajima variety is canonically isomorphic to the coordinate ring of the scheme-theoretic fixed locus of the stability cocharacter on the flavor-deformed Coulomb branch. This is an isomorphism of graded algebras over the common coefficient ring, retaining nilpotents and including the empty case.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Hikita’s conjecture relates two different kinds of algebra: the cohomology of a symplectic resolution and the functions on a fixed subscheme of a corresponding Coulomb branch. The scheme structure is essential. Even when the underlying fixed-point set is small, its nilpotent functions can encode positive-degree cohomology. For quiver gauge theories, the tautological classes on the Higgs side and the equivariant parameters on the Coulomb side give a common coefficient ring. The question is whether the relations among those coefficients agree.

We prove this agreement for arbitrary finite quivers, under the precise regularity hypothesis stated below. The proof keeps the coefficient maps and the full fixed schemes throughout. Its two main comparisons have different purposes: a calculation for the full topological torus identifies the cohomology algebra, while a separate argument identifies that torus-fixed scheme with the fixed scheme of the stability cocharacter.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The statement and its coefficient maps

<a id="eq:gauge-data"></a>
Let $Q=(Q_0,Q_1)$ be a finite oriented quiver. Loops and multiple arrows are allowed. Fix complex vector spaces $V_i,W_i$ of dimensions $v_i,w_i$, and put $$\label{eq:gauge-data}
 G=\prod_{i\in Q_0}\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V_i),\qquad
 N=\bigoplus_{a:i\to j}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(V_i,V_j)
       \oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_0}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(V_i,W_i).$$ An algebraic torus $F$ acts linearly on $N$ and commutes with $G$; write $\mathfrak f=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}F$. The induced action on $T^*N=N\oplus N^*$ is Hamiltonian. For $(s,p)\in N\oplus N^*$, our cotangent moment map is characterized by $\langle\mu(s,p),\xi\rangle=\langle p,\xi s\rangle$. Choose a character $\nu$ of $G$ for which the $\nu$-semistable locus in $\mu^{-1}(0)$ equals the stable locus and $G$ acts freely there. We call such a character *regular*, and set <a id="eq:higgs-definition"></a>$$\label{eq:higgs-definition}
 D=\mu^{-1}(0)^{\nu\text{-stable}},\qquad X=D/G.$$ Thus $X$ is a smooth Nakajima variety. We allow $D$ and $X$ to be empty.

Put $\mathcal O=\mathbb C[[t]]$ and $\mathcal K=\mathbb C((t))$. The Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima space of triples and its flavor-deformed Coulomb algebra are <a id="eq:triples-definition"></a><a id="eq:coulomb-definition"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal R&=\{[g,s]\in G(\mathcal K)\times_{G(\mathcal O)}N(\mathcal O):gs\in N(\mathcal O)\},
       \label{eq:triples-definition}\\
 A_F&=H_*^{G(\mathcal O)\times F}(\mathcal R;\mathbb C),\qquad
 Y_{\mathfrak f}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A_F.\label{eq:coulomb-definition}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $(g,s)\sim(gh^{-1},hs)$, and the multiplication is BFN convolution. We use the commutative algebra, without the loop-rotation deformation parameter. Its topological grading is by $\pi_1(G)=\mathbb Z^{\{i:v_i>0\}}$, and defines a torus $T_{\mathrm{top}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\pi_1(G),\mathbb C^\times)$. The character $\nu$ gives a cocharacter of this torus. Its fixed locus is scheme-theoretic: if $(A_F)_m$ denotes its weight-$m$ subspace, then <a id="eq:nu-quotient"></a>$$\label{eq:nu-quotient}
 \mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}]
       =A_F/\langle(A_F)_m:m\ne0\rangle.$$ No reduction of this quotient will be taken.

Let $T\subset G$ be the diagonal maximal torus, let $\mathcal W$ be its Weyl group, and write <a id="eq:coefficient-ring"></a>$$\label{eq:coefficient-ring}
 R=H^*_{G\times F}(\mathrm{pt};\mathbb C)
   =\mathbb C[\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}T\oplus\mathfrak f]^{\mathcal W},\qquad
 R_0=H_G^*(\mathrm{pt};\mathbb C).$$ The Kirwan map $\phi_H:R\to H_F^*(X;\mathbb C)$ and the coefficient map $\phi_C:R\to\mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}]$ are the natural maps on the two sides of the Hikita conjecture. On the first side, a gauge Chern class maps to the Chern class of its tautological bundle, and a flavor parameter acts through $H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})$. On the second side, $R$ acts by the coefficient classes of the Coulomb algebra, followed by the fixed-scheme quotient. Each linear gauge or flavor parameter has degree two.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every datum <a href="#eq:gauge-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gauge-data">[eq:gauge-data]</a>, every commuting flavor torus $F$, and every regular stability character $\nu$, both coefficient maps are surjective and have the same kernel. They therefore induce a canonical isomorphism of graded $R$-algebras $$H_F^*(X;\mathbb C)\ \cong\ \mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}].$$ The gradings are ordinary cohomological degree and BFN homological degree, respectively. The assertion includes the empty case, whose ring is zero.*

</div>

This is the equivariant cohomological Hikita conjecture for quiver gauge theories, in the form of (Dumanski and Krylov 2025, Conjecture 2.1, Equation (2.3)) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)]. It concerns the full coefficient-algebra quotient, with its nilpotents. No finite-type, Dynkin, conical, or good/ugly hypothesis is imposed.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and the comparison problem

A classical model is the description of type-$A$ Springer-fiber cohomology through scheme-theoretic intersections of nilpotent orbit closures with a Cartan subalgebra, developed by de Concini–Procesi and Tanisaki (Concini and Procesi 1981; Tanisaki 1982) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DeConciniProcesi81](#ref-DeConciniProcesi81), [Tanisaki82](#ref-Tanisaki82)]. In the setting of symplectic duality (Braden et al. 2016) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BLPW16](#ref-BLPW16)], Hikita formulated a fixed-scheme realization of cohomology and established it for the Hilbert scheme of points in the plane, with related type-$A$ Spaltenstein and smooth hypertoric examples (Hikita 2017) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Hikita17](#ref-Hikita17)]. These examples explain why the infinitesimal structure of the fixed locus, rather than its set of points, is central to the question.

Nakajima proposed a mathematical Coulomb-branch construction for three-dimensional gauge theories (Nakajima 2016) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Nakajima16](#ref-Nakajima16)]; Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima constructed its commutative convolution algebra (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, 2019) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18), [BFN19](#ref-BFN19)], extending the affine-Grassmannian convolution framework of Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg–Mirković (Bezrukavnikov, Finkelberg, and Mirković 2005) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BFM05](#ref-BFM05)]. For quiver gauge theories the corresponding Higgs quotient is a Nakajima variety (Nakajima 1994) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Nakajima94](#ref-Nakajima94)]. The coefficient-compatible formulation has important predecessors. Kamnitzer–Tingley–Webster–Weekes–Yacobi record Nakajima’s equivariant conjecture in the affine-Grassmannian setting and strengthen its specialized form by requiring compatibility with tautological classes (Kamnitzer et al. 2019, Conjectures 8.9–8.10) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KTWWY19](#ref-KTWWY19)]. Hilburn–Kamnitzer–Weekes formulate the equivariant quantized refinement over the common coefficient algebra (Hilburn, Kamnitzer, and Weekes 2023, sec. 5.7, Conjecture 5.26) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HKW23](#ref-HKW23)]. Krylov–Shlykov state the general-quiver comparison for the *full topological-torus fixed scheme* as equality of coefficient-map kernels (Krylov and Shlykov 2025, sec. 8.1, Conjecture 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[KrylovShlykov25](#ref-KrylovShlykov25)]. We first establish that equality and then compare the full-torus fixed scheme with the stability-cocharacter fixed scheme. Thus compatibility with the coefficient maps is part of the conjectural formulation itself.

Krylov–Shlykov prove the Gieseker, or framed Jordan-quiver, case (Krylov and Shlykov 2025) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[KrylovShlykov25](#ref-KrylovShlykov25)]. Dumanski–Krylov give the formulation used here and compare cohomological, equivariant, and $K$-theoretic versions (Dumanski and Krylov 2025) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)]. Their finite-type conclusion includes types $A,D,E_6$; in types $E_7,E_8$ it imposes the framing restrictions in (Dumanski and Krylov 2025, Corollary 2.17) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)]. Their Remark 2.18 explains why the earlier unrestricted ADE assertion must be qualified. Recent developments also include Setiabrata’s Hikita surjectivity result for the base-affine-cotangent example (Setiabrata 2024) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Setiabrata24](#ref-Setiabrata24)], and the refined quantum Hikita theorem of Dinkins–Karpov–Krylov for ADE quivers with minuscule-supported framing and for the Jordan quiver (Dinkins, Karpov, and Krylov 2026, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DinkinsKarpovKrylov26](#ref-DinkinsKarpovKrylov26)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> treats the cohomological algebra for all the finite quiver data above. It does not assert the quantized statement in (Dumanski and Krylov 2025, Equation (2.2)) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)], nor extend the theorem to arbitrary reductive gauge groups; outside this setting, proposed Hikita-type identifications can fail (Hoang, Krylov, and Matvieievskyi 2024, Example 6.7) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HoangKrylovMatvieievskyi24](#ref-HoangKrylovMatvieievskyi24)].

Several established tools enter the argument in distinct ways. BFN abelianization turns minuscule convolution classes into explicit Laurent expressions, and Weekes’s generation theorem makes these classes sufficient for arbitrary quivers (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, 2019; Weekes 2019) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18), [BFN19](#ref-BFN19), [Weekes19](#ref-Weekes19)]. McGerty–Nevins’s ordinary and equivariant Kirwan surjectivity results supply the tautological presentation on the Higgs side (McGerty and Nevins 2018, Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MN18](#ref-MN18)]. Hausel’s arithmetic Fourier-transform formula computes the Betti numbers that eventually determine the size of the coefficient quotient (Hausel 2006) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Hausel06](#ref-Hausel06)]. Character-linearized GIT and the topological Kempf–Ness correspondence connect regular stability to the flat hyperkähler quotient construction (King 1994; Hoskins 2014; Hitchin et al. 1987; Nakajima 1994) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[King94](#ref-King94), [Hoskins14](#ref-Hoskins14), [HKLR87](#ref-HKLR87), [Nakajima94](#ref-Nakajima94)]. Our use of these inputs is explained at their precise interfaces below.

The finite-dimensional critical-model viewpoint is related to Nakajima’s original construction and to Botta–Tamagni’s critical lattice and quasimap models (Nakajima 2016; Botta and Tamagni 2026) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Nakajima16](#ref-Nakajima16), [BottaTamagni26](#ref-BottaTamagni26)]. Here the specific stable-pair test proves an inclusion of coefficient ideals; it does not require their entire Coulomb-action construction. Deformation arguments also appear in Krylov–Shlykov’s Gieseker proof (Krylov and Shlykov 2025, Remark 1.15) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[KrylovShlykov25](#ref-KrylovShlykov25)]. Our loop-removal step uses subgroup localization (Brion 1998) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Brion98](#ref-Brion98)], with the prequotient perspective of (Dumanski and Krylov 2025, Proposition 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)], and an explicit descent for homogeneous ideals. These connections distinguish the established methods from the particular comparison arguments carried out here.

The difficulty is not just to match dimensions. One must identify the relations under the specified maps, with their grading and nilpotents, for arbitrary flavors and for every regular stability. Neither a calculation on closed fixed points nor an isomorphism after inverting generic parameters establishes that assertion. A further distinction is that a regular character can satisfy $\nu\cdot\mathbf b=0$ for an integer vector $0<\mathbf b\leq\mathbf v$. Therefore the full topological-torus calculation does not by itself identify the cocharacter-fixed quotient.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy and organization

Define the intermediate coefficient algebra and the two ideals to be compared by <a id="eq:intro-two-ideals"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-two-ideals}
 \begin{split}
 B_F&=\mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{T_{\mathrm{top}}}]
      =A_F/\langle A_F^{(d)}:d\ne0\rangle,\\
 I_{\mathrm{top}}&=\ker(R\longrightarrow B_F),\qquad
 I_H=\ker(R\xrightarrow{\phi_H}H_F^*(X)),
 \end{split}$$ where $A_F^{(d)}$ is the subspace of topological charge $d\in\pi_1(G)$; its cocharacter weight is $\nu\cdot d$. Neither of these is the cohomological degree used in Hilbert series. Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> explains why the coefficient maps to $B_F$ and to $H_F^*(X)$ are surjective. Thus the first task is precisely the equality $I_{\mathrm{top}}=I_H$. The second task is the equality of the cocharacter and full-torus defining ideals inside $A_F$. A subscript $0$ means that the flavor torus is trivial, so $R_0=H_G^*(\mathrm{pt})$ and $B_0$ denotes the corresponding full-torus quotient. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-route" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-route">1</a> separates the two tasks.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### 1. Stable pairs give a quotient map.

Sections <a href="#sec:correspondences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correspondences">3</a> and <a href="#sec:stable-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:stable-pairs">4</a> prove $I_{\mathrm{top}}\subseteq I_H$ for all the data of the theorem. The nontrivial minuscule generators used here are dressed lattice modifications whose coweight entries are all in $\{0,1\}$ or all in $\{0,-1\}$, with nonzero topological charge; a subset may involve several vertices. A convolution word is a finite product of these classes. By minuscule generation, a coefficient in $I_{\mathrm{top}}$ is a finite sum of positive-length convolution words, each of total topological charge zero. Section <a href="#sec:correspondences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correspondences">3</a> realizes each such word by finite-dimensional lattice correspondences, keeping all degree shifts. The test in Section <a href="#sec:stable-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:stable-pairs">4</a> evaluates them on stable cotangent pairs. The stability character assigns an additive degree to each modification, so the degrees in a neutral word sum to zero. After reordering each word, its first modification is nontrivial and has nonpositive character-line degree; stability excludes it. On the identity correspondence, the same test gives a split restriction onto $H_F^*(X)$, which detects the coefficient class itself. The tested relation therefore vanishes in cohomology, yielding the graded coefficient-compatible surjection $B_F\twoheadrightarrow H_F^*(X)$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-inclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-inclusion">8</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### 2. Added loops make the size comparison explicit.

Temporarily suppose that the quiver has a loop at every vertex of positive gauge dimension, take no flavor, and use determinant stability $\nu_{\det}=\prod_i\det_{V_i}$. The loop weights cancel the Grassmannian denominators in minuscule abelianization. Section <a href="#sec:laurent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:laurent">5</a> proves a Laurent model with polynomial coefficients and filters $B_0$ by multipartitions of the gauge dimension vector. Its successive quotients are bounded by invariant parts of polynomial quotients defined by cut products: products of framing variables and differences across a subset of slots and its complement. Section <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">6</a> computes their Hilbert series using a reachability stratification and identifies the resulting sum with Hausel’s ordinary Poincaré polynomial. The upper bound and the surjection from the first step force equality in every degree. This is an isomorphism under the original coefficient map, not merely a numerical identity; see Corollary <a href="#cor:loop-isomorphism" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:loop-isomorphism">20</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### 3. Deformation returns to the original data.

For an arbitrary quiver $Q$, let $Q^+$ be obtained by adding one loop at each vertex of positive gauge dimension. Give the new loops the common weight one of an auxiliary flavor torus $H=\mathbb C^\times$, with parameter $z\in H_H^2(\mathrm{pt})$. The comparison in Section <a href="#sec:deformations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:deformations">7</a> uses a lift of the no-flavor isomorphism for $Q^+$ to this flavor. Near zero gauge parameters and nonzero mass $z=1$, the new minuscule Euler factors are invertible on the Coulomb side. Subgroup localization on the stable prequotient gives the corresponding comparison on the Higgs side. The resulting equality of localized coefficient ideals descends to the original homogeneous ideals in $R_0$: their equality at the graded origin determines them globally. This homogeneity argument, not specialization of an unspecified generic isomorphism, is the content of Proposition <a href="#prop:loop-removal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:loop-removal">23</a>. Local constancy of regular stability, quaternionic rotation, and generic constancy of compactly supported Betti numbers then compare all regular characters. A final flavor lift proves $B_F\simeq H_F^*(X)$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:full-torus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:full-torus">24</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### 4. Complementary monopoles identify the cocharacter quotient.

Section <a href="#sec:cocharacters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cocharacters">8</a> proves, independently of the preceding cohomology comparison, that the natural map $\mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}]\to B_F$ is an isomorphism. It combines polynomial-form Hall-algebra generation (Jindal and Neguţ 2026) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[JindalNegut26](#ref-JindalNegut26)] with the monopole maps of Lemma <a href="#lem:polynomial-monopole-maps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:polynomial-monopole-maps">25</a>, derived from (Tsymbaliuk 2023, Theorem 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Tsymbaliuk23Difference](#ref-Tsymbaliuk23Difference)] with explicit product signs. The resulting finite identities can be specialized without dividing by rotation or arrow parameters. After adjoining a rank-one framing vertex, any surviving nontrivial minuscule monopole has a surviving complementary monopole. Hall generation would then decompose both charges into indecomposable charges of the enlarged quiver, all of weight zero for the extended character. Lemma <a href="#lem:root-moment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:root-moment">27</a> realizes these charges by moment-level blocks. Their semistable direct sum has a stabilizer that remains positive-dimensional even after removing the ineffective diagonal scalar. This contradicts the freeness in regularity, including when the semistable locus is empty. Proposition <a href="#prop:cocharacter-fixed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cocharacter-fixed">28</a> therefore identifies the two defining ideals, completing the scheme-theoretic comparison.

 \mathbb C[Y_{\mathfrak f}^{\nu}]\ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Coefficient rings, minuscule classes, and flavor

This section supplies the generation and coefficient maps needed for the two ideal comparisons described in Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a>. Minuscule generation makes $B_F$ a quotient of $R$; Kirwan surjectivity does the same for $H_F^*(X)$. Flavor base change and local constancy of regular stability will let us apply these presentations to the auxiliary data used later.

<a id="eq:full-fixed-quotient"></a>
We use complex coefficients throughout. Vertices with $v_i=0$ can be discarded. If all $v_i$ vanish, both sides of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> equal $H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})$, so we henceforth allow ourselves to omit that case. Write $$P_F=\mathbb C[x_{ia}:i\in Q_0,\ 1\le a\le v_i]\otimes\mathbb C[\mathfrak f],
 \qquad \mathcal W=\prod_i S_{v_i},\qquad R=P_F^{\mathcal W}.$$ The $x_{ia}$ are the Chern roots of the gauge bundles. Each linear form in $P_F$ has cohomological degree two. An element $\lambda=(\lambda_{ia})\in X_*(T)$ has topological charge $[\lambda]=(\sum_a\lambda_{ia})_i$. We write $A_F^{(d)}$ for the subspace of charge $d$ and define $$\label{eq:full-fixed-quotient}
 B_F=A_F/\langle A_F^{(d)}:d\ne0\rangle.$$ Thus $B_F$ is the coordinate ring of the fixed subscheme for $T_{\mathrm{top}}$. A subscript $0$ means that the flavor torus is trivial.

<a id="stmt-lem-trivial-summands"></a>
<div id="lem:trivial-summands" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *For the purposes of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, one may assume that the flavor action assigns a character to each arrow summand and acts diagonally on each framing multiplicity space. Adding or removing a $G$-trivial representation with any flavor action preserves both coefficient-algebra quotients and all stability conditions.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-trivial-summands"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trivial-summands</summary>

*Proof.* Decompose $N$ into $G$-isotypic summands. A commuting torus acts on each multiplicity space and is diagonalizable there. The nontrivial irreducible gauge representations that occur are the bifundamentals between distinct vertices, the dual standard representations from framing, and the traceless endomorphisms at vertices of rank at least two. All remaining summands are gauge-trivial. A weighted traceless endomorphism summand can be replaced by the full endomorphism representation by adding a scalar line of the same flavor weight. This gives the asserted description.

If $E$ is gauge-trivial, the moment map for $N\oplus E$ is the moment map for $N$, and semi-invariant generation shows that the stable and semistable loci are the corresponding loci for $N$, times $T^*E$. Their stabilizers are unchanged. The quotient gains a factor $T^*E$, which retracts $F$-equivariantly to zero. In the space of triples the condition on the $E(\mathcal O)$ coordinate imposes no lattice constraint, so this coordinate gives a common vector-space factor in every finite truncation. The normalized equivariant Thom isomorphism removes this factor, compatibly with convolution, both gradings, and the coefficient map. This proves the claim on both sides. ◻

</details>

We call a coweight *positive minuscule* if all its entries, across all vertices, are $0$ or $1$, and *negative minuscule* if all entries are $0$ or $-1$. Its orbit $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^\lambda$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G=G(\mathcal K)/G(\mathcal O)$ is a product of ordinary Grassmannians and is closed and smooth.

Write $\mathcal W_\lambda=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathcal W}(\lambda)$, $u_+=\max(u,0)$, and let $\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(N)$ denote the multiset of weights of $N$. For $p\in P_F^{\mathcal W_\lambda}$, put $p_{w\lambda}=w(p)$; stabilizer invariance makes this independent of the representative $w$.

<a id="stmt-prop-minuscule"></a>
<div id="prop:minuscule" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:minuscule"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Abelianization and minuscule generation). *There is an injective coefficient-preserving algebra map from $A_F$ into $$\bigl(\operatorname{Frac}(P_F)[X_*(T)]\bigr)^{\mathcal W},\qquad
 e^\lambda e^\gamma=e^{\lambda+\gamma}.$$ Besides $R$, the algebra is generated by the dressed classes associated with all nonzero positive and negative minuscule coweights. For $p\in P_F^{\mathcal W_\lambda}$, the class has expression $$\label{eq:minuscule}
 \sum_{\lambda'\in\mathcal W\lambda}
 e^{\lambda'}p_{\lambda'}
 \frac{\prod_{\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(N)}
           \beta^{(-\langle\beta,\lambda'\rangle)_+}}
      {\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}_T(T_{t^{\lambda'}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^\lambda)}.$$ Each distinct translate occurs once, and the dressing is translated with the coweight. Denote this class by $M_{\lambda,p}$. Weights $\beta$ include their flavor components, but pairings use only the gauge components; factors with exponent zero are omitted. The class has charge $[\lambda]$. On charge zero, the BFN grading is twice the ordinary polynomial degree of the rational coefficients in this description.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-minuscule"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-minuscule</summary>

*Proof.* We use the classical BFN localization and flavor construction (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, Proposition 3.22, Lemmas 5.9–5.13, Proposition 5.15, and Corollary 5.21) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18)], and its minuscule formula (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, Proposition 6.6) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18)] <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[BFN19](#ref-BFN19)](Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2019, Appendix A, Proposition A.2), together with quiver minuscule generation (Weekes 2019, Proposition 3.1 and Remarks 3.3–3.4) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Weekes19](#ref-Weekes19)]. The form of the formula can be checked directly: the inclusion of the space of triples into the bundle of lattices $gN(\mathcal O)$ contributes the Euler class of $$t^{\lambda'}N(\mathcal O)\big/
       \bigl(N(\mathcal O)\cap t^{\lambda'}N(\mathcal O)\bigr),$$ which is the numerator of <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a>. Localization on the closed minuscule orbit gives the tangent denominator.
