There is no rotation, so translating a modification by $t^\eta$ changes the Laurent monomial but does not shift the coefficients. The localization theorem and the orbit filtration give injectivity before taking Weyl invariants. Equivalently, the image of the nonabelian algebra lies in the invariant part of the displayed ring.

For completeness, the minuscule coweights used in the generation theorem are subsets of slots at several vertices at once. Decomposing positive or negative integral coweights by successive level sets gives exactly these two families; no assertion that single-slot monopoles suffice is needed. Lemma <a href="#lem:trivial-summands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:trivial-summands">2</a> puts arbitrary commuting flavors in weighted quiver form. Flavor characters change the equivariant Euler factors but not the gauge-weight hyperplane arrangement used in the generation proof; the same chamber argument therefore retains those parameters, as discussed in (Weekes 2019, sec. 1.6.1) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Weekes19](#ref-Weekes19)]. The embedding used here is the classical localization map, not a specialization of a representation in which loop rotation is inverted. Finally, put $c_\lambda=\sum_{\beta\in\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(N)}
(-\langle\beta,\lambda\rangle)_+$ and $a_\lambda=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^\lambda$. For homogeneous $p$, the BFN homological normalization gives $$\deg M_{\lambda,p}=2c_\lambda-2a_\lambda+\deg p$$ by (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.5–2.6) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18)]. These are exactly the degrees of the numerator, tangent denominator, and dressing in Equation <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a>. The bare matter-free torus class $e^\lambda$ has degree zero. This proves the stated grading comparison. General dressings are decomposed into homogeneous summands. The additional shifts from a common finite section cutoff will be kept explicitly in Section <a href="#sec:correspondences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correspondences">3</a>. No nonnegative grading on $A_F$ itself is assumed. ◻

</details>

Every nontrivial minuscule generator in Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a> has nonzero charge. Consequently the coefficient map $R\to B_F$ is surjective, and $B_F$ is nonnegatively graded. More precisely, the ideal in <a href="#eq:full-fixed-quotient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-fixed-quotient">[eq:full-fixed-quotient]</a> is generated by those minuscule classes: after killing them the algebra is generated by $R$ and is supported in charge zero. In particular, a coefficient relation in $B_F$ can be expressed as a finite sum of neutral words of positive length in the nonzero minuscule generators, with coefficients in $R$.

<a id="stmt-lem-flavor-base-change"></a>
<div id="lem:flavor-base-change" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Flavor base change). *Let $F\to F'$ be a homomorphism of algebraic tori, and suppose the action on $N$ is obtained by restriction of an $F'$-action commuting with $G$. Then the natural maps are isomorphisms $$\begin{aligned}
 A_{F'}\otimes_{H_{F'}^*(\mathrm{pt})}H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})&\ \cong A_F,\\
 B_{F'}\otimes_{H_{F'}^*(\mathrm{pt})}H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})&\ \cong B_F.
\end{aligned}$$ They respect the coefficient maps, both gradings, and convolution.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-flavor-base-change"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-flavor-base-change</summary>

*Proof.* Filter the affine Grassmannian by bounded unions of orbits and truncate all section lattices between sufficiently high positive and negative powers of $t$. Over an orbit, the truncated space of triples is a vector bundle of constant relative rank. The orbit is an affine-space bundle over a partial flag variety; its equivariant homology, with the relative shifts in the definition of $A_F$, is even. Its $F'$-equivariant homology is free over $H_{F'}^*(\mathrm{pt})$, and restricting the flavor action gives the displayed change of coefficients on this stratum. The exact sequences for adjoining strata have no odd terms, so these statements extend through each finite filtration. The free successive quotients also show that tensoring the sequences introduces no Tor term. Passing to the stabilized truncations and their exhaustive union proves the first assertion. Pullback, refined intersection, and proper pushforward defining convolution commute with this change of equivariance, so the assertion is an algebra isomorphism.

Flavor parameters have charge zero. Under the first isomorphism the ideal generated by all nonzero charge spaces is the extension of the same ideal before base change. Taking the quotient therefore gives the second assertion; no flatness of $B_{F'}$ is required for this step. ◻

</details>

For the moment-map comparisons below, we use the topological affine Kempf–Ness theorem: the quotient of a character-shifted real moment level by a compact form of $G$ is homeomorphic to the corresponding affine-character GIT quotient (Hoskins 2014, Theorem 4.2(v)) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Hoskins14](#ref-Hoskins14)]. The same statement restricts to a closed invariant subvariety. Indeed, its GIT quotient embeds as a closed subspace of the ambient quotient, and its orbit closures and their closed representatives remain in that subvariety. This applies in particular to every central complex moment fiber in $T^*N$.

<a id="stmt-lem-local-stability"></a>
<div id="lem:local-stability" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Local constancy of regular stability). *Let $\theta$ be a rational character parameter for which every semistable point of $\mu^{-1}(0)$ is stable and has trivial stabilizer. There is a neighborhood $U$ of $\theta$ in $X^*(G)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R$ such that the stable and semistable loci are unchanged for every rational $\theta'\in U$. One may choose such a $\theta'$ outside all proper hyperplanes $\theta'\cdot\mathbf b=0$ with $0<\mathbf b\leq\mathbf v$, and then clear its denominators by a positive integer. Empty loci are included.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-local-stability"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-local-stability</summary>

*Proof.* We identify a positive multiple of a rational character with the same stability condition. Fix a maximal torus of $G$ and the finite set of its weights on $N\oplus N^*$. For a point $x$ and $g\in G$, let $C(gx)$ be the cone generated by the weights occurring in $gx$. The affine Hilbert–Mumford criterion says that $x$ is semistable precisely when the character belongs to every such cone, and is stable with finite stabilizer precisely when it belongs to all their full interiors in the real weight space; see (King 1994) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[King94](#ref-King94)] and (Hoskins 2014, Propositions 2.5 and 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Hoskins14](#ref-Hoskins14)]. Although $g$ varies, only finitely many cones occur. A sufficiently small neighborhood of $\theta$ remains inside each cone whose interior contains $\theta$, and outside each cone that does not contain $\theta$. A $\theta$-stable point therefore remains stable, and a $\theta$-unstable point remains unstable. Regularity accounts for all points of the closed subvariety $\mu^{-1}(0)$. This proves the assertion about the entire loci, including when they are empty. The geometric quotient and its principal gauge bundle are unchanged, as is freeness. Choose a rational point in this neighborhood avoiding the finitely many proper hyperplanes specified in the statement, and then rescale by a positive integer. If $G$ is trivial, its character space is a point and the list of bounded nonzero dimension vectors is empty, so the assertion is immediate. ◻

</details>

The Kirwan map $R\to H_F^*(X)$ is surjective. For nonempty $X$, Lemma <a href="#lem:local-stability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-stability">5</a> permits a rational perturbation $\theta$ of $\nu$ that preserves the entire stable locus $D$ and avoids $\theta\cdot\mathbf b=0$ for $0<\mathbf b\leq\mathbf v$. Its extension $(\theta,-\theta\cdot\mathbf v)$ is nondegenerate for the framed dimension vector $(\mathbf v,1)$: a proper subvector has final coordinate zero, or its nonzero complement does. Clearing denominators preserves stability. The quotient, its principal gauge bundle, and its tautological bundles are unchanged, so ordinary Kirwan surjectivity applies with the same coefficient map (McGerty and Nevins 2018, Definition 3.1 and Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[MN18](#ref-MN18)]; see also (Dumanski and Krylov 2025, sec. 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[DK25](#ref-DK25)]. The torus-equivariant conclusion is (McGerty and Nevins 2018, Corollary 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[MN18](#ref-MN18)]; we recall its argument for an arbitrary commuting flavor torus. Choose a homogeneous basis of $H^*(X)$ represented by polynomials in the ordinary tautological Chern classes. These classes lift to $F$-equivariant Chern classes. Leray–Hirsch for $X\to EF\times_F X\to BF$ therefore makes $H_F^*(X)$ free over $H_F^*(\mathrm{pt})$ on these lifts and proves the asserted equivariant surjectivity. In particular $H^*(X)$ is finite-dimensional and even. For empty $X$ the assertion means the unique surjection to the zero ring.

<a id="sec:correspondences"></a>
### A finite correspondence test for Coulomb relations

We work with the finite-dimensional algebraic group $\mathsf K=G\times F$. All homology in this section is equivariant Borel–Moore homology, with complex coefficients. We use constructible sheaves only on finite-dimensional varieties. The purpose of the section is to express a coefficient relation in the Coulomb algebra as a relation between such sheaves.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Bounded lattice families

<a id="eq:finite-lattice-space"></a>
<a id="eq:lattice-rank"></a>
A point $b=gG(\mathcal O)$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G$ determines the lattice $gN(\mathcal O)$ in $N(\mathcal K)$. For a BFN triple $[g,s_0]$, we use $n=gs_0$ as section coordinate; the fiber over $b$ is $N(\mathcal O)\cap gN(\mathcal O)$. Let $B$ be a projective $\mathsf K$-variety equipped with a $\mathsf K$-equivariant map to $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G$ with bounded image. The map need not be an embedding. For any finite collection of these families, choose $l$ large enough that all their lattices contain $t^lN(\mathcal O)$ and are contained in $t^{-l}N(\mathcal O)$. Set $$\label{eq:finite-lattice-space}
 \begin{aligned}
 L&=t^{-l}N(\mathcal O)/t^lN(\mathcal O),&
 E_b&=gN(\mathcal O)/t^lN(\mathcal O),\\
 S_B&=\{(b,n)\in B\times L:n\in E_b\},&
 f_B&:S_B\longrightarrow L.
 \end{aligned}$$ Here and below the same notation is used for a lattice and for its pullback to a parameter space. The representation map $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(N)}$ identifies $S_B$ with the pullback of a tautological vector bundle on a finite Grassmannian. We work separately on components if its rank varies. If $\chi_N=\det(N|_G)$, its rank on a component of degree $\gamma\in\pi_1(G)$ is $$\label{eq:lattice-rank}
 r_B=l\dim N-\langle\chi_N,\gamma\rangle.$$ The incidence $S_B\subset B\times L$ is closed, so $f_B$ is proper. We write $S_{\mathrm{pt}}=N(\mathcal O)/t^lN(\mathcal O)$ for the family at the identity lattice and $i=f_{\mathrm{pt}}$. Put $r=l\dim N$ and $\mathcal F_B=Rf_{B,*}\mathbb C_{S_B}$; in particular, $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{pt}}=i_*\mathbb C_{S_{\mathrm{pt}}}$.

Let $Z$ be a finite union of affine Schubert varieties in the neutral component, containing the identity lattice. Restriction to that lattice gives a degree-zero morphism <a id="eq:restriction-at-identity"></a>$$\label{eq:restriction-at-identity}
 \rho_Z:\mathcal F_Z\longrightarrow\mathcal F_{\mathrm{pt}}.$$ Both section bundles here have rank $r$. The base $Z$ is allowed to be singular.

The notation $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^d_{\mathsf K,L}(\mathcal F,\mathcal G)$ denotes $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal F,\mathcal G[d])$ for $\mathsf K$-equivariant constructible complexes on $L$, computed on the common Borel approximations used below.

<a id="stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization"></a>
<div id="lem:correspondence-factorization" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:neutral-word-relation"></a>
<a id="eq:finite-relation-factorization"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Finite correspondence factorization). *Let $u\in R$ be homogeneous of cohomological degree $d$, and suppose that in $A_F$ it has an expression $$\label{eq:neutral-word-relation}
 u=\sum_w M_{\lambda_{w,1},p_{w,1}}\cdots
                 M_{\lambda_{w,k_w},p_{w,k_w}},
 \qquad k_w\geq1,
 \qquad \sum_j[\lambda_{w,j}]=0\in\pi_1(G).$$ Every factor is a nontrivial dressed positive or negative minuscule generator of Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>, and every word is homogeneous of degree $d$. Coefficients in $R$ may be included in the dressings. Choose an ordering of the factors of each word. There are $Z$ as above bounding their final lattices and a common truncation <a href="#eq:finite-lattice-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-lattice-space">[eq:finite-lattice-space]</a> such that $$\label{eq:finite-relation-factorization}
 u\rho_Z=\sum_w\Psi_w
 \quad\text{in}\quad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^d_{\mathsf K,L}
       (\mathcal F_Z,\mathcal F_{\mathrm{pt}}).$$ Each $\Psi_w$ factors through a cohomological shift of $\mathcal F_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_{w,1}}}$. Only the endpoints are required to lie in $Z$ or in the neutral component; all intermediate lattices are included in the common truncation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-correspondence-factorization</summary>

*Proof.* We first specify the correspondences and their degrees, then prove the identity by a faithful localization calculation.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
###### Correspondences and shifts.

<a id="eq:finite-correspondence-Hom"></a>
For proper maps $f:X\to L$ and $g:Y\to L$, write $\omega_Y$ for the complex-linear dualizing complex of $Y$. Proper base change and adjunction give $$R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_L(Rf_*\mathbb C_X,Rg_*\omega_Y)
 \simeq R\Gamma(X\times_LY,\omega_{X\times_LY}).$$ For example, if $q:X\times_LY\to Y$ is the projection, the left side is $R\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_Y(q_*\mathbb C_{X\times_LY},\omega_Y)$, which gives the displayed identity by $q_*\dashv q^!$. Thus, when $Y$ is smooth of complex dimension $e$, we have $$\label{eq:finite-correspondence-Hom}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^a_{\mathsf K,L}(Rf_*\mathbb C_X,Rg_*\mathbb C_Y)
 \simeq H^{\mathsf K}_{2e-a}(X\times_LY).$$ The equivariant version follows on common Borel approximations; their additional dimensions cancel in the shift. These finite-type models have triangulable analytic topology and finite cohomological dimension. We use the proper-support base-change and adjunction formalism of (Schnürer and Soergel 2016, Theorems 5.10 and 7.7) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[SchnurerSoergel16](#ref-SchnurerSoergel16)], with complex coefficients, and the duality and Borel–Moore identifications of (Maxim and Schürmann 2021, Example 2.7(3) and Corollary 2.11) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[MaximSchurmann21](#ref-MaximSchurmann21)]. In particular, the source $X$ need not be smooth. Composition across a smooth middle space is refined intersection along its diagonal followed by proper pushforward. These operations are the adjunction and base-change operations in the displayed identification.

Fix one word, temporarily suppressing its index $w$. Let $B_j$ be the variety of chains of lattices with successive relative positions $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_j$, starting at the identity. Set $B_0=\mathrm{pt}$. Each forgetful map $B_j\to B_{j-1}$ is a bundle with fiber the closed minuscule orbit $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_j}$, so the $B_j$ are smooth and projective. Write $$a_j=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_j},\qquad
 r_j=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}E_{b_j},\qquad
 I_j=\{(b_0,\ldots,b_j,n):n\in E_{b_{j-1}}\cap E_{b_j}\}.$$ The intersection has constant rank by fixed relative position. It defines a vector bundle on $B_j$ and a correspondence $$S_{B_j}\ \overset{v_j}{\hookleftarrow}\ I_j
       \xrightarrow{u_j} S_{B_{j-1}}$$ over $L$, where $v_j$ is the closed subbundle inclusion and $u_j$ forgets the last lattice. Write $c_j=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(E_{b_j}/I_j)$, using the same symbol $I_j$ for the intersection bundle. The dressing $p_j$ gives an equivariant characteristic class on $B_j$: use the two tautological blocks of the minuscule modification in the preceding lattice frame, together with the flavor classes. This is the twisted-orbit version of the stabilizer-invariant dressing in <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a>.

If $\delta_j=\deg p_j$, the class $p_j\cap[I_j]$ defines a morphism $\mathcal F_{B_j}\to\mathcal F_{B_{j-1}}[q_j]$, where <a id="eq:chain-shift"></a>$$\label{eq:chain-shift}
 q_j=2(r_{j-1}-r_j+c_j-a_j)+\delta_j.$$ Indeed, $\dim I_j=\dim B_{j-1}+a_j+r_j-c_j$, and <a href="#eq:finite-correspondence-Hom" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-correspondence-Hom">[eq:finite-correspondence-Hom]</a> uses the target dimension $\dim S_{B_{j-1}}=\dim B_{j-1}+r_{j-1}$. The endpoint map $B_k\to Z$ induces a proper map $S_{B_k}\to S_Z$. Its pullback morphism on constant sheaves, followed by pushforward to $L$, gives $\mathcal F_Z\to\mathcal F_{B_k}$ with no shift. Compose it with the $k$ incidence morphisms to obtain $\Psi_w$. This already gives the stated first-step factorization.

At a modification of type $\lambda_j$, $c_j=\sum_\beta(-\langle\beta,\lambda_j\rangle)_+$, with multiplicities and with the pairing taken on the gauge part. The rational coefficient in Equation <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a> has degree $2c_j-2a_j+\delta_j$, counting linear forms with degree two. For the neutral word, the sum of these degrees is its BFN degree $d$, by Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>. Moreover, Equation <a href="#eq:lattice-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lattice-rank">[eq:lattice-rank]</a> gives $r_k=r_0=r$. Consequently $$\sum_jq_j
 =2(r_0-r_k)+\sum_j(2c_j-2a_j+\delta_j)=d.$$ Thus $\Psi_w$ and $u\rho_Z$ belong to the same graded Hom space. No equality of intermediate ranks or identification of nonneutral degree conventions is used.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
###### Faithfulness of the localization test.

Put $C_Z=S_{\mathrm{pt}}\times_LS_Z$ and let $\iota:C_Z\hookrightarrow
S_Z$ be the inclusion. The identity <a href="#eq:finite-correspondence-Hom" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-correspondence-Hom">[eq:finite-correspondence-Hom]</a>, with smooth target $S_{\mathrm{pt}}$, identifies the Hom space in <a href="#eq:finite-relation-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-relation-factorization">[eq:finite-relation-factorization]</a> with $H^{\mathsf K}_{2r-d}(C_Z)$. Under it, $\rho_Z$ is the fundamental class of $S_{\mathrm{pt}}$ over the identity lattice.

Let $P=H^*_{T\times F}(\mathrm{pt})$. The stratification of $C_Z$ over the finitely many $G(\mathcal O)$-orbits in $Z$ has vector-bundle strata. Each orbit is an affine-space bundle over a partial flag variety and contracts to it under loop scaling $t\mapsto at$ as $a\to0$. This contraction commutes with $T$, and $F$ acts trivially on the orbit. Its ordinary equivariant cohomology is therefore even and free over $P$; smooth equivariant Poincaré duality gives the same assertion for Borel–Moore homology, with a dimension shift. The section bundle adds an even Thom shift. The closed-stratum exact sequences split into short exact sequences by parity, and their free quotients make each extension free over $P$. It follows that $H_*^{T\times F}(C_Z)$ is free, in particular torsion-free. This is the finite version of the orbit-filtration argument in (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, Lemmas 2.5–2.6) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18)].

The $T$-fixed lattices in $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G$ are precisely the diagonal lattices $t^\eta$: a fixed lattice in each $V_i(\mathcal K)$ splits along its distinct diagonal characters, and a lattice in a one-dimensional Laurent series space is $t^m\mathcal O$. At $t^\eta$, every $(T\times F)$-weight-zero summand of $N$ has gauge pairing zero with $\eta$. Its lattice is unchanged by intersection with $N(\mathcal O)$. Thus $\iota$ identifies the fixed loci of $C_Z$ and $S_Z$. Equivariant localization makes $\iota_*$ an isomorphism after tensoring with $\operatorname{Frac}P$; torsion-freeness makes this localized pushforward a faithful test on $H_*^{T\times F}(C_Z)$. Passing to Weyl invariants recovers $\mathsf K$-equivariance. This is also the fixed-fiber calculation of (Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima 2018, Lemma 5.13) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[BFN18](#ref-BFN18)].

After localization, the classes of the entire section fibers $E_{t^\eta}$ form a basis for the homology of $S_Z$, indexed by its fixed lattices. These classes, rather than isolated points in a fiber, allow weight-zero summands of $N$ without inverting a zero Euler class. We denote this basis formally by $e^\eta$.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
###### The chain calculation.

<a id="eq:chain-euler-product"></a>
For each partial chain put $C_j=S_{\mathrm{pt}}\times_LS_{B_j}$, and let $h_j:C_j\hookrightarrow S_{B_j}$ forget the initial section constraint. Let $z_j$ be the Borel–Moore class of the composite $\mathcal F_{B_j}\to\mathcal F_{\mathrm{pt}}[\sum_{h=1}^j q_h]$ formed by the first $j$ incidence morphisms. Since $S_{B_j}$ is smooth, write $(h_j)_*z_j=\alpha_j\cap[S_{B_j}]$. We claim that $$\label{eq:chain-euler-product}
 \alpha_k=\prod_{j=1}^k p_j\,\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}(E_{b_j}/I_j),$$ with all factors pulled to $B_k$.

The initial class has $\alpha_0=1$. At the $j$th step, composition is refined intersection along the diagonal of the smooth middle space $S_{B_{j-1}}$. This intersection commutes with the proper pushforward that forgets the initial section constraint. We may therefore first replace $z_{j-1}$ by $(h_{j-1})_*z_{j-1}
=\alpha_{j-1}\cap[S_{B_{j-1}}]$. The remaining intersection pulls this cohomology class back to $I_j$, giving $$(h_j)_*z_j
 =(v_j)_*\bigl(u_j^*\alpha_{j-1}\,p_j\cap[I_j]\bigr).$$ Here the regular diagonal belongs to the smooth middle space; no smoothness of $u_j$ is needed. The classes $\alpha_{j-1}$ are pulled from the chain bases, and the subbundle inclusion $v_j$ multiplies by $\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}(E_{b_j}/I_j)$. This proves <a href="#eq:chain-euler-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-euler-product">[eq:chain-euler-product]</a> by induction.

Under the same Hom–Borel–Moore identification, precomposition with the unit for the proper endpoint map $S_{B_k}\to S_Z$ is proper pushforward along $C_k\to C_Z$. Forgetting the initial section constraint commutes with this pushforward. Thus the endpoint pushforward of $\alpha_k\cap[S_{B_k}]$ is exactly the image of $\Psi_w$ in the localization test.

Fixed chains are sequences of Weyl translates $\lambda'_j$ of the chosen minuscule coweights; their successive lattices are $t^{\eta_j}$ with $\eta_j=\sum_{h\leq j}\lambda'_h$. At such a chain the $j$th modification is translated by $t^{\eta_{j-1}}$. This translation commutes with the diagonal torus. There is no loop rotation, so it changes neither the relevant weights nor the dressing values. Hence its quotient Euler class is $$\prod_\beta\beta^{(-\langle\beta,\lambda'_j\rangle)_+},$$ and its relative tangent Euler class is $\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}_T(T_{t^{\lambda'_j}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_j})$. Localization on the smooth chain base therefore sends <a href="#eq:chain-euler-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-euler-product">[eq:chain-euler-product]</a>, after endpoint pushforward, to $$\sum_{(\lambda'_1,\ldots,\lambda'_k)}
 e^{\lambda'_1+\cdots+\lambda'_k}
 \prod_{j=1}^k
 \left(
 p_{j,\lambda'_j}
 \frac{\prod_\beta\beta^{(-\langle\beta,\lambda'_j\rangle)_+}}
 {\mathop{\mathrm{eu}}_T(T_{t^{\lambda'_j}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G^{\lambda_j})}
 \right).$$ This is exactly the product of the expressions <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a> for the word. All dressings are transported with their coweights. The fiber ranks at the endpoint equal $r$, so no additional fiber factor enters endpoint pushforward.

The localized pushforward of $u\rho_Z$ is $u e^0$. Equation <a href="#eq:neutral-word-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:neutral-word-relation">[eq:neutral-word-relation]</a> and the abelianization of Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a> identify it with the sum of the displayed word expressions. The faithful test just proved implies the equality in $H^{\mathsf K}_{2r-d}(C_Z)$ and hence <a href="#eq:finite-relation-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-relation-factorization">[eq:finite-relation-factorization]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

**Remark 7**. Every $u\in\ker(R\to B_F)$ admits an expression of the form <a href="#eq:neutral-word-relation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:neutral-word-relation">[eq:neutral-word-relation]</a>, degree by degree. Indeed, Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a> implies that the ideal of all nonzero topological degrees is generated by the nontrivial positive and negative minuscule generators. Expand a relation in that ideal and retain its neutral component. Commutativity of $A_F$ permits any desired ordering of the factors before applying Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a>.

</div>

The relation has now been transferred to a finite-dimensional category, with the identity-lattice map on one side and first-step factorizations on the other. The stable-pair test in Section <a href="#sec:stable-pairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:stable-pairs">4</a> will annihilate the selected first-step objects while keeping a split restriction from the identity-lattice test onto cohomology.

<a id="sec:stable-pairs"></a>
### Testing coefficient relations by stable pairs

We use the finite-dimensional correspondences of Section <a href="#sec:correspondences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:correspondences">3</a> to prove

<a id="stmt-prop-kernel-inclusion"></a>
<div id="prop:kernel-inclusion" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:stable-pair-surjection"></a>
**Proposition 8**. *For every datum and regular stability in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the coefficient map induces a graded $R$-algebra surjection $$\label{eq:stable-pair-surjection}
 B_F\twoheadrightarrow H_F^*(X).$$*

</div>

Throughout this section, $\mathsf K=G\times F$. We retain the notation $L$, $E_b$, $S_B$, $f_B$, $i=f_{\mathrm{pt}}$, and $\rho_Z$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a>. All cohomology in the test below is ordinary equivariant cohomology.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### A degree constraint on global stable pairs

We first record the geometric consequence of stability that will exclude the first lattice of each word. For a principal $G$-bundle $\mathcal P$ on $\mathbb P^1$, write $\mathcal L_\nu$ for its character line bundle, with the convention that evaluating a $\nu^m$-semi-invariant on a section of an associated representation gives a section of $\mathcal L_\nu^{\otimes m}$. Its degree is a linear function of the topological class of $\mathcal P$.

<a id="stmt-lem-stable-section-degree"></a>
<div id="lem:stable-section-degree" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *Let $\mathcal P$ be a principal $G$-bundle on $\mathbb P^1$, equipped with a frame at infinity, and let $\sigma$ be a global section of $\mathcal P\times_G(T^*N)$. Suppose that its value $\sigma_\infty$ in this frame is $\nu$-stable and has trivial stabilizer. Then $\deg\mathcal L_\nu\geq0$. If $\deg\mathcal L_\nu=0$, the pair $(\mathcal P,\sigma)$ has a unique trivialization, agreeing with the given frame at infinity, in which $\sigma$ is the constant section $\sigma_\infty$.*

<a id="eq:based-loop-trivialization"></a>
*In particular, suppose that $\mathcal P$ is specified by a lattice $b\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}_G$, with its usual trivialization on $\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0\}$. In the degree-zero case its topological class is zero and $$\label{eq:based-loop-trivialization}
 b=hG(\mathcal O),\qquad
 h\in G(\mathbb C[t^{-1}]),\qquad h(\infty)=1.$$ In the off-zero frame, the section is $h\sigma_\infty$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-stable-section-degree"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-stable-section-degree</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a $\nu^m$-semi-invariant $f$, with $m>0$, that is nonzero at $\sigma_\infty$. Its evaluation on $\sigma$ is a global section of a positive power of $\mathcal L_\nu$, nonzero at infinity. This proves nonnegativity of the degree.

If the degree is zero, this section is nowhere zero. Thus $\sigma$ is everywhere semistable and determines a morphism $$\gamma:\mathbb P^1\longrightarrow (T^*N)/\!/_{\nu}G.$$ Every invariant polynomial evaluated on $\sigma$ is a regular function on $\mathbb P^1$, hence constant. The image of $\gamma$ therefore lies in one fiber of the projective morphism to the affine quotient $(T^*N)/\!/G$. A sufficiently divisible GIT polarization is relatively ample and pulls back to a positive power of $\mathcal L_\nu$. Its degree is zero, so $\gamma$ is constant: an ample line bundle has positive degree on the image of a nonconstant morphism from $\mathbb P^1$. These are the usual character-linearized affine GIT constructions; see (King 1994, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[King94](#ref-King94)].

The constant value is the stable quotient point represented by $\sigma_\infty$. Its fiber in the semistable locus is the single orbit $G\sigma_\infty$. Consequently $\sigma$ is a section of the associated orbit bundle $\mathcal P\times_G(G\sigma_\infty)$. Since $G\sigma_\infty\simeq G$ equivariantly, this section trivializes $\mathcal P$. Freeness makes the trivialization unique once the coordinates of the section are prescribed to be $\sigma_\infty$; it agrees with the specified frame at infinity.

In the lattice presentation, compare this global trivialization with the fixed trivialization off zero. Their transition is a regular map $\mathbb A^1_{t^{-1}}\to G$ equal to $1$ at infinity. It is the loop $h$ in <a href="#eq:based-loop-trivialization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:based-loop-trivialization">[eq:based-loop-trivialization]</a>, and the lattice of regular sections at zero is $hG(\mathcal O)$. ◻

</details>

Every point of $D=\mu^{-1}(0)^{\nu\text{-stable}}$ is stable with trivial stabilizer also in the ambient representation $T^*N$. Indeed, for a closed invariant subvariety, semi-invariants lift by exactness of reductive invariants, and orbit closures stay in that subvariety. The affine GIT stability criterion therefore agrees with its ambient restriction. Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-section-degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-section-degree">9</a> applies to the *pair* $(s,p)\in D$.

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### The finite vanishing-cycle test

<a id="eq:stable-test-function"></a>
For the truncation parameter $l$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a>, put $$L_-=\bigoplus_{j=-l}^{-1}t^jN,\qquad
 L_+=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{l-1}t^jN,\qquad
 P_-=\bigoplus_{j=-l}^{-1}t^jN^*.$$ Thus $L=L_-\oplus L_+$, and the residue pairing identifies $P_-$ with $L_+^*$. Regard $s\in N$ and $p\in N^*$ as constant series. On $\widetilde L=L\times D\times P_-$ define the $\mathsf K$-invariant function $$\label{eq:stable-test-function}
 W(n,s,p,y)=
 \mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\bigl(\langle p+y,n\rangle-\langle y,s\rangle\bigr),
 \qquad (s,p)\in D.$$ After restricting $n$ to a moving lattice, variation in $y$ imposes the prescribed nonnegative modes $\operatorname{pr}_{L_+}n=s$, while variation in $n$ imposes dual-lattice membership for $p+y$. These are the extension conditions for the two components of the stable pair across the lattice modification. The residue is the coefficient of $t^{-1}$; the expression is well-defined on the truncated spaces.

<a id="eq:stable-test-proper"></a>
Use the shifted vanishing-cycle functor $\Phi_f=\phi_f[-1]$, normalized so that $\Phi_0$ is the identity. Vanishing cycles will be regarded as complexes on the ambient space by closed direct image from the zero fiber. The test of a complex $\mathcal F$ on $L$ is <a id="eq:stable-test-functor"></a>$$\label{eq:stable-test-functor}
 \mathsf T(\mathcal F)
 =R\Gamma_{\mathsf K}\!
 \left(\widetilde L,\,
 \Phi_W\,\operatorname{pr}_L^*\mathcal F\right).$$ For a bounded projective lattice family $B$, set $M_B=S_B\times D\times P_-$, and let $W_B$ be the pullback of $W$. Proper base change and compatibility of vanishing cycles with proper direct image give $$\label{eq:stable-test-proper}
 \mathsf T(f_{B,*}\mathbb C_{S_B})
 \simeq R\Gamma_{\mathsf K}
       (M_B,\Phi_{W_B}\mathbb C_{M_B}).$$ Here proper-direct-image compatibility is (Maxim and Schürmann 2021, Proposition 4.19(1)) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[MaximSchurmann21](#ref-MaximSchurmann21)]; the smooth projection pullback uses part (2). The same operations in the critical-cohomology setting are recalled in (Botta and Tamagni 2026, Appendix A.1) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[BottaTamagni26](#ref-BottaTamagni26)].

The test is $R$-linear. To see this precisely, work on a finite Borel approximation with base $B\mathsf K$. A coefficient class $u$ is a morphism $\mathbb C_{B\mathsf K}\to\mathbb C_{B\mathsf K}[\deg u]$, pulled back to each space. The projection formula makes proper direct image compatible with this action (Schnürer and Soergel 2016, Theorem 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[SchnurerSoergel16](#ref-SchnurerSoergel16)]. Nearby cycles commute naturally with tensoring by a locally constant complex pulled back from the base; taking the specialization cone gives the same assertion for vanishing cycles. Naturality with respect to the morphism representing $u$ therefore identifies its tested action with cup product by the restricted class. Passing to sufficiently large approximations proves the assertion for $R=H_{\mathsf K}^*(\mathrm{pt})$.

<a id="stmt-lem-stable-test-support"></a>
<div id="lem:stable-test-support" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10**. *The support of $\Phi_{W_B}\mathbb C_{M_B}$ is contained in the closed locus $\Sigma_B$ defined by <a id="eq:stable-critical-equations"></a>$$\label{eq:stable-critical-equations}
 n\in s+L_-,
 \qquad
 p+y\in gN^*(\mathcal O)/t^lN^*(\mathcal O),
 \qquad b=gG(\mathcal O).$$ Here $n\in E_b$ is already part of the definition of $M_B$. If $B$ lies in a nonzero topological component whose character-line degree is nonpositive, then $\Sigma_B$ is empty and the test in <a href="#eq:stable-test-proper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-test-proper">[eq:stable-test-proper]</a> is zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-stable-test-support"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-stable-test-support</summary>

*Proof.* Differentiating in $y$ gives $\operatorname{pr}_{L_+}n=s$. Differentiating in the section variable $n\in E_b$ says that $p+y$ annihilates $E_b$ in the residue pairing. The annihilator is the displayed truncated dual lattice. These conditions are zeros of algebraic bundle maps and hence define a closed locus.

This derivative calculation gives a support bound even when $B$ is singular. Off $\Sigma_B$, a derivative in one of the vector-bundle fiber coordinates is nonzero. The function is affine linear in that coordinate; where its coefficient is invertible, replacing the coordinate by $W_B$ gives a product chart over the unchanged base. The constant sheaf has zero vanishing cycles in this chart.

A bounded lattice in each general linear factor specifies a modification of the trivial vector bundle at zero, determined by finite jets. A point of $\Sigma_B$ gives two global sections on $\mathbb P^1$ for the resulting principal bundle. In the off-zero frame they are the polynomials $n$ and $p+y$ in $t^{-1}$, with values $s$ and $p$ at infinity. The lattice conditions are exactly their extendability at zero: the discarded $t^l$ tails already lie in the corresponding lattices. Their value at infinity is the stable pair $(s,p)$. Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-section-degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-section-degree">9</a> now excludes negative character-line degree and, in degree zero, forces the entire topological class to be zero. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### The neutral component and its quadratic normal form

Let $Z$ be the neutral projective Schubert union used in Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a>, containing the base lattice $1$. Set $r=l\dim N$. Since the index of the associated representation lattice factors through $\pi_1(G)$, the bundles $E_b$ have rank $r$ on $Z$. Define the invariant open subset <a id="eq:stable-test-open"></a>$$\label{eq:stable-test-open}
 U=\{b\in Z:\operatorname{pr}_{L_+}:E_b\xrightarrow{\sim}L_+\}.$$ It contains $1$.

<a id="stmt-lem-quadratic-test"></a>
<div id="lem:quadratic-test" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles"></a>
<a id="eq:quadratic-restriction"></a>
**Lemma 11**. *Assume $D\neq\varnothing$. The locus $\Sigma_Z$ is a closed graph $\Gamma\simeq U\times D$ in $M_Z$. There are compatible $\mathsf K$-equivariant identifications $$\label{eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles}
 \Phi_{W_Z}\mathbb C_{M_Z}
 \simeq \iota_{\Gamma,*}\mathbb C_\Gamma[-2r],
 \qquad
 \Phi_{W_{\mathrm{pt}}}\mathbb C_{M_{\mathrm{pt}}}
 \simeq \iota_{D,*}\mathbb C_D[-2r].$$ Under these identifications, the test of $\rho_Z$ induces ordinary restriction $$\label{eq:quadratic-restriction}
 H_{\mathsf K}^*(U\times D)\longrightarrow H_{\mathsf K}^*(D)$$ with the same uniform shift on source and target. This map is split surjective and $R$-linear.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-test"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-quadratic-test</summary>

*Proof.* First, every point of $\Sigma_Z$ lies over $U$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-section-degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-section-degree">9</a>, its principal bundle is trivial, with a based loop $h$ as in <a href="#eq:based-loop-trivialization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:based-loop-trivialization">[eq:based-loop-trivialization]</a>. A vector in the kernel of $E_b\to L_+$ is a negative polynomial section of its associated $N$-bundle, extending at zero and vanishing at infinity. That associated bundle is trivial, so the vector is zero. The two spaces have dimension $r$, and the projection is an isomorphism.

<a id="eq:stable-quadratic-coordinates"></a>
Over $U$, write $E_b$ as the graph of a regular linear map $A_b:L_+\to L_-$. Thus $n=a+A_ba$ for $a\in L_+$. Define the linear functional $$\ell_{b,p}(a)=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\langle p,A_ba\rangle.$$ Via $P_-=L_+^*$, Equation <a href="#eq:stable-test-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-test-function">[eq:stable-test-function]</a> becomes $$\label{eq:stable-quadratic-coordinates}
 W_Z=\langle y,a-s\rangle+\ell_{b,p}(a).$$ Its fiber critical equations have the unique solution $a=s$, $y=-\ell_{b,p}$, depending regularly on $(b,s,p)$. Consequently $\Sigma_Z$ is the graph $\Gamma\simeq U\times D$. It is closed in all of $M_Z$ because the equations defining $\Sigma_Z$ are closed and every solution has already been shown to lie over $U$.

It remains to compute the critical value. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-section-degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-section-degree">9</a> to this solution. In the off-zero frame, $$n=hs,\qquad p+y=hp.$$ Invariance of the pairing gives $\langle hp,hs\rangle=\langle p,s\rangle$, a constant series. Hence $$W_{\mathrm{crit}}
 =-\langle[t^{-1}](hp),s\rangle.$$ Write $h=1+\xi t^{-1}+O(t^{-2})$, where $\xi\in\mathfrak g$. The coefficient $[t^{-1}](hp)$ is the infinitesimal dual action $\xi\cdot p$. Therefore <a id="eq:stable-critical-value"></a>$$\label{eq:stable-critical-value}
 W_{\mathrm{crit}}
 =-\langle\xi\cdot p,s\rangle
 =\langle p,\xi\cdot s\rangle
 =\langle\mu(s,p),\xi\rangle=0.$$ Thus the regular, equivariant translations $a'=a-s$, $y'=y+\ell_{b,p}$ turn <a href="#eq:stable-quadratic-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-quadratic-coordinates">[eq:stable-quadratic-coordinates]</a> into the fixed split form $$W_Z=\langle y',a'\rangle$$ over $U\times D$.

The vanishing cycles of this form on $L_+\oplus L_+^*$ are one copy of $\mathbb C[-2r]$ at the origin. The external-product theorem applies to the constant sheaf of $U\times D$ even if $U$ is singular (Massey 2001, Theorem, pp. 1–2 of the arXiv preprint) <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[Massey99](#ref-Massey99)]. The splitting is global and equivariant. Its structure group acts as $g\oplus g^{-T}$, so the quadratic vanishing-cycle line has trivial equivariant monodromy. Choosing its identification with $\mathbb C$ once gives compatible identifications over the whole base.

Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-test-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-test-support">10</a> says that the vanishing cycles are zero off the closed graph $\Gamma$. The product calculation therefore gives the first isomorphism in <a href="#eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles">[eq:quadratic-vanishing-cycles]</a> on all of $M_Z$. In particular, there are no further cycles over $Z\setminus U$: every point there fails at least one of the fiber equations. The same computation at $1$ has $A_1=0$, $\ell_{1,p}=0$, and the same $2r$ quadratic variables, giving the second isomorphism.

The map $\rho_Z$ is restriction to the base lattice. To identify its test as a morphism, not just its source and target, put $B=U\times D$ and consider the maps $$D\xrightarrow{\,j\,}U\times D\xrightarrow{\,\pi\,}D,
 \qquad j(d)=(1,d),\quad \pi(b,d)=d.$$ These maps are $\mathsf K$-equivariant and satisfy $\pi j=\mathrm{id}$. Their ordinary cohomological pullbacks satisfy $j^*\pi^*=\mathrm{id}$. On the same base $B$, apply the natural external-product identification to the morphism $\mathbb C_B\to Rj_*\mathbb C_D$, with zero function on $B$ and the fixed split quadratic on $L_+\oplus L_+^*$. Since $j$ is closed, proper-direct-image compatibility identifies the target with the quadratic calculation over $D$. One common choice of generator of the quadratic line then identifies the resulting morphism with $\mathbb C_B[-2r]\to Rj_*\mathbb C_D[-2r]$. Taking ordinary equivariant cohomology gives exactly Equation <a href="#eq:quadratic-restriction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quadratic-restriction">[eq:quadratic-restriction]</a>. This uses naturality in a sheaf morphism and proper direct image, not commutation with an arbitrary nonsmooth pullback. The identity $j^*\pi^*=\mathrm{id}$ supplies the $R$-linear splitting, without any properness requirement on $\pi$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-test-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-quadratic-test</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-inclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-inclusion">8</a>.* The assertion is immediate if $D=\varnothing$, so assume $D$ is nonempty. Let $u\in R$ be homogeneous and zero in $B_F$. Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a> expresses $u$ in $A_F$ as a finite sum of positive-length words in nontrivial dressed positive and negative minuscule generators, with coefficients absorbed into the dressings. Taking the full topological-degree-zero part, every word has total topological degree zero.

The character-line degrees of its steps sum to zero. Choose a step with nonpositive character-line degree and, by commutativity, put it first. Its topological degree is nonzero. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a>, with a common neutral endpoint bound $Z$. The morphism $u\rho_Z$ is a sum of compositions, each factoring through the first-step object $f_{B,*}\mathbb C_{S_B}$, with the prescribed shifts. By Lemma <a href="#lem:stable-test-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stable-test-support">10</a>, the test of every such object is zero. It follows that $$\mathsf T(u\rho_Z)=0.$$

The degree calculation in Lemma <a href="#lem:correspondence-factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:correspondence-factorization">6</a> identifies the degree of this morphism with $\deg u$, including when intermediate lattice components are nonneutral. The test commutes with shifts, and Lemma <a href="#lem:quadratic-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:quadratic-test">11</a> places the same quadratic shift on its source and target. Its $R$-linearity therefore gives the equality of graded maps $$(u|_D)\smile j^*=0.$$ Apply this equality to $\pi^*(1)$. Since $j^*\pi^*(1)=1$, we obtain $u|_D=0$ in $H_{\mathsf K}^*(D)$.

The free stable-locus action identifies $H_{\mathsf K}^*(D)=H_F^*(X)$, compatibly with the coefficient map. We have proved $$\ker(R\to B_F)\subseteq\ker(R\to H_F^*(X)).$$ Both rings are generated by their coefficient images. For $B_F$ this follows from Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>; for $H_F^*(X)$ it follows from Kirwan surjectivity (McGerty and Nevins 2018) <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[MN18](#ref-MN18)] and its equivariant extension recalled above. The displayed inclusion induces the graded $R$-algebra surjection <a href="#eq:stable-pair-surjection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-pair-surjection">[eq:stable-pair-surjection]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:laurent"></a>
### A Laurent model and a filtration by multipartitions

To complement the surjection of Proposition <a href="#prop:kernel-inclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:kernel-inclusion">8</a>, we seek an upper bound for the Hilbert series of $B_0$. One loop at each vertex cancels the minuscule tangent denominators, giving a Laurent model for this calculation.

Throughout this Section the flavor torus is trivial, and the quiver has at least one loop at every vertex of positive gauge dimension. Delete the vertices of gauge dimension zero, and choose one loop at every remaining vertex. Let $Q'$ be the quiver obtained by deleting these chosen loops. Write $$n_{ij}=\#Q'_1(i,j),\qquad c_{ij}=n_{ij}+n_{ji},\qquad
 C(\mathbf v)=\frac12\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}v_iv_j.$$ In particular, $c_{ii}=2n_{ii}$. We use the set $$\Omega=\{(i,a):i\in Q_0,\ 1\leq a\leq v_i\}$$ of gauge slots, the polynomial ring $P=\mathbb C[x_{ia}:(i,a)\in\Omega]$, and $\mathcal W=\prod_iS_{v_i}$. Let $\mathcal B'$ be the multiset consisting of the weights $x_{jb}-x_{ia}$ of all arrows of $Q'$ and $w_i$ copies of $-x_{ia}$ at each gauge slot. The zero weights arising from diagonal entries of loops can be omitted: their coweight pairing is always zero.

For $\lambda\in\mathbb Z^\Omega$, put $$E_\beta(\lambda)=\bigl(-\langle\beta,\lambda\rangle\bigr)_+,
 \qquad
 r_\lambda=e^\lambda\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal B'}
                      \beta^{E_\beta(\lambda)},$$ where $u_+=\max(u,0)$ and $e^\lambda e^\gamma=e^{\lambda+\gamma}$. These expressions belong to $\mathbb C(x_{ia})[\mathbb Z^\Omega]$. For a polynomial $p\in P^{\mathcal W_\lambda}$, with $\mathcal W_\lambda=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathcal W}(\lambda)$, denote the distinct-translate orbit sum by $$\mathsf S_\lambda(p)=
 \sum_{w\in\mathcal W/\mathcal W_\lambda}w(p r_\lambda).$$ The choice of representative of the coweight orbit does not affect the space of such sums.

<a id="stmt-prop-laurent-model"></a>
<div id="prop:laurent-model" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:laurent-model"></a>
**Proposition 12**. *Under the abelianization embedding of Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>, the no-flavor Coulomb algebra is $$\label{eq:laurent-model}
 A_0=\left(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\mathbb Z^\Omega}P r_\lambda\right)^{\mathcal W}.$$ The identification preserves the coefficient ring $R_0=P^{\mathcal W}$ and the topological grading.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-laurent-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-laurent-model</summary>

<a id="eq:laurent-product"></a>
*Proof.* First, the direct sum on the right is an algebra. Indeed, $$\label{eq:laurent-product}
 r_\alpha r_\gamma
 =r_{\alpha+\gamma}
  \prod_{\beta\in\mathcal B'}
   \beta^{E_\beta(\alpha)+E_\beta(\gamma)-E_\beta(\alpha+\gamma)},$$ and every exponent is nonnegative. The multiset $\mathcal B'$ is $\mathcal W$-stable, so taking invariants gives a subalgebra.

For a positive or negative subset coweight, the weights of the deleted loop at a vertex cancel the tangent Euler denominator in Equation <a href="#eq:minuscule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minuscule">[eq:minuscule]</a>, up to a nonzero constant sign. This is the Grassmannian tangent-weight calculation: for a positive subset $I$, the loop contributes $\prod_{a\in I,\ b\notin I}(x_{ib}-x_{ia})$, while the tangent weights are these roots, up to their common sign convention. For a negative subset the roots are reversed. This is the local Euler-factor calculation associated with minuscule flag localization; compare the pushforward identity in (Weekes 2019, Lemma 2.10) <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[Weekes19](#ref-Weekes19)]. Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a> therefore places every generator of $A_0$ in the right-hand side of Equation <a href="#eq:laurent-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laurent-model">[eq:laurent-model]</a>. Conversely, it places every $\mathsf S_\alpha(p)$ for a positive or negative subset coweight $\alpha$ in $A_0$, since a constant sign can be removed.

We prove that all the orbit sums $\mathsf S_\lambda(p)$ belong to $A_0$ by strong induction on $$\|\lambda\|^2=\sum_{(i,a)\in\Omega}\lambda_{ia}^2.$$ The coefficient and positive or negative subset-coweight cases have just been established. If $\lambda$ has a positive coordinate, set $$f(t)=1_{t>0},\qquad g(t)=t-1_{t>0};$$ otherwise set $$f(t)=-1_{t<0},\qquad g(t)=t+1_{t<0}.$$ In either case define $\alpha=f(\lambda)$ and $\gamma=g(\lambda)$ coordinatewise. Outside the base cases both $\alpha$ and $\gamma$ have squared norm strictly smaller than that of $\lambda$. Both functions are nondecreasing on $\mathbb Z$, and their values have compatible signs relative to zero. Thus, for every pair of slots $d,e$ and every slot $d$, $$(\alpha_d-\alpha_e)(\gamma_d-\gamma_e)\geq0,
 \qquad \alpha_d\gamma_d\geq0.$$ These statements apply across different colors as well. Consequently every exponent in Equation <a href="#eq:laurent-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laurent-product">[eq:laurent-product]</a> is zero for this pair, and $r_\alpha r_\gamma=r_\lambda$.

Order the slots at each vertex by their $\lambda$-coordinates. Then $\alpha$ and $\gamma$ are ordered in the same way. The rearrangement inequality gives, for every relative matching $w\in\mathcal W$, $$\|\alpha+w\gamma\|^2
 \leq\|\alpha+\gamma\|^2=\|\lambda\|^2.$$ In the equality case, the resulting sum lies in the orbit of $\lambda$. To see the last assertion directly, sort the matched entries within each color. Swapping an inverted pair strictly increases the scalar product unless one of the two corresponding pairs is tied; swaps involving ties leave the multiset of sums unchanged. Hence every maximal matching has the same multiset of sums as the ordered matching. All other orbits occurring in a product $\mathsf S_\alpha(p_\alpha)\mathsf S_\gamma(p_\gamma)$ therefore have strictly smaller squared norm.

We now verify that the leading coefficient can be an arbitrary $\mathcal W_\lambda$-invariant dressing. At the fixed representative $\lambda$, the decomposition into translates of $\alpha$ and $\gamma$ is unique. For example, in the positive case, a different assignment of the $1$-entries of $\alpha$ would assign $1$ to some coordinate $\lambda_d\leq0$ and $0$ to some coordinate $\lambda_e>0$ of the same color. The corresponding $\gamma$-entries would satisfy $\gamma'_d=\lambda_d-1<\lambda_e=\gamma'_e$, which is a strict inversion relative to $\alpha'$. This contradicts maximality. The negative case would similarly assign $0$ to a negative coordinate $\lambda_d$ and $-1$ to a nonnegative coordinate $\lambda_e$ of the same color; then $\gamma'_d=\lambda_d<\lambda_e+1=\gamma'_e$ is again a strict inversion relative to $\alpha'$. Thus the coefficient of $r_\lambda$ in that product is precisely $p_\alpha p_\gamma$.

Within each color, the level blocks of $\alpha$ and $\gamma$ are intervals in the chosen ordering. Their combined cuts are exactly the cuts of the level blocks of $\lambda$: two distinct values cannot have both the same $f$-value and the same $g$-value, since $f(t)+g(t)=t$. For every $k\geq1$, the power sum on each $\lambda$-block is therefore a linear combination of power sums on $\alpha$-blocks and $\gamma$-blocks. Explicitly, obtain prefix power sums at all cuts from whichever block partition contains the cut, and subtract successive prefixes. Power sums generate each symmetric-polynomial ring over $\mathbb C$, so $$P^{\mathcal W_\lambda}
 =\bigl\langle P^{\mathcal W_\alpha},
                 P^{\mathcal W_\gamma}\bigr\rangle.$$ As the rings commute, every element of the ring on the right is a finite sum of products $p_\alpha p_\gamma$ from the two indicated subrings. Such products of dressed orbit sums realize any prescribed leading dressing. The other terms have polynomial dressings by Equation <a href="#eq:laurent-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laurent-product">[eq:laurent-product]</a>; their dressings are invariant under the stabilizers of their coweights because the full product is $\mathcal W$-invariant. The induction hypothesis removes all those lower terms. This proves the reverse inclusion, without inverting any polynomial, and completes the proof. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### Multipartitions and cut ideals

For the rest of this Section, $q$ records half the cohomological degree: each polynomial variable has cohomological degree two and contributes $q$. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}(M;q)=\sum_d\dim(M^{2d})q^d$ for the evenly graded vector spaces under consideration.

<a id="eq:cut-polynomial"></a>
Let $\mathcal M(\mathbf v)$ be the finite set of arrays $$m=(m_{i,s})_{i\in Q_0,\ s\geq1},\qquad
 m_{i,s}\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0},\qquad \sum_{s\geq1}s m_{i,s}=v_i.$$ For $m\in\mathcal M(\mathbf v)$, set $$l_i=\sum_{s\geq1}m_{i,s},\qquad
 \mathbf l=(l_i)_i,\qquad |l|=\sum_i l_i,$$ and $$D_m=\frac12\sum_{i,j}\sum_{s,t\geq1}
                c_{ij}\min(s,t)m_{i,s}m_{j,t}.$$ Let $\Omega_m^+$ consist of $m_{i,s}$ distinguished copies of each type $(i,s)$. These are the *positive slots*. For $d\in\Omega_m^+$ denote its color by $i(d)$ and its size by $s(d)$. Define $$P_m=\mathbb C[x_d:d\in\Omega_m^+],\qquad
 \mathcal W_m=\prod_{i,s}S_{m_{i,s}},\qquad
 b_{de}=c_{i(d)i(e)}\min(s(d),s(e)).$$ The group $\mathcal W_m$ permutes copies of the same type. For every nonempty subset $S\subseteq\Omega_m^+$, put $$\label{eq:cut-polynomial}
 q_S=
 \prod_{d\in S}x_d^{w_{i(d)}}
 \prod_{\substack{d\in S\\ e\in\Omega_m^+\setminus S}}
                    (x_d-x_e)^{b_{de}}.$$ Let $J_m\subseteq P_m$ be the ideal generated by these cut polynomials, and set $$h_m(q)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}\bigl((P_m/J_m)^{\mathcal W_m};q\bigr).$$ The ideal is homogeneous and $\mathcal W_m$-stable. For the empty array, these conventions give $P_m=\mathbb C$, $J_m=0$, and $h_m(q)=1$.

These cut products have a familiar graphical interpretation. Adjoin a framing vertex $0$, put $w_{i(d)}$ edges between $0$ and each positive slot $d$, and put $b_{de}$ edges between each unordered pair of distinct slots $d,e$. When this multigraph is connected, $J_m$ is the cocircuit ideal of its reduced incidence configuration, with the row for $0$ deleted. A minimal disconnecting cut contributes the product of its edge forms, $x_d$ for framing edges and $x_d-x_e$ for slot edges; every cut product is divisible by a minimal-cut product. Such ideals occur in toric hyperkähler cohomology (Hausel and Sturmfels 2002, sec. 7, Equation (37)) <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[HauselSturmfels02](#ref-HauselSturmfels02)] and in permutation-invariant quiver constructions (Reineke, Rhoades, and Tewari 2023, sec. 2 and 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[ReinekeRhoadesTewari23](#ref-ReinekeRhoadesTewari23)]. A component disjoint from $0$ instead gives a unit cut and zero quotient. Section <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">6</a> gives a direct reachability calculation that retains the $\mathcal W_m$-action and yields the recurrence needed here.

<a id="stmt-prop-filtration-bound"></a>
<div id="prop:filtration-bound" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:filtration-bound"></a>
**Proposition 13**. *The quotient $B_0$ for the full topological torus satisfies the coefficientwise graded bound $$\label{eq:filtration-bound}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}(B_0;q)\ \leq
 \sum_{m\in\mathcal M(\mathbf v)}
 q^{C(\mathbf v)-D_m+\mathbf w\cdot(\mathbf v-\mathbf l)}h_m(q).$$ Every exponent displayed in the sum is a nonnegative integer.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-filtration-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-filtration-bound</summary>

*Proof.* Choose, for each color, an identification of its $l_i$ positive slots with $l_i$ of its $v_i$ gauge slots. On the remaining $v_i-l_i$ slots put $s(d)=0$; call these the *zero slots* and write their set as $\Omega_m^0$. Define a coweight by $$(\lambda_m)_d=s(d)-1\qquad(d\in\Omega).$$ The word “positive” refers to the part size, so at a rank-three vertex the sizes $(2,1,0)$ give the coweight $(1,0,-1)$. The coweight $\lambda_m$ has topological degree zero, since the sum of its coordinates at vertex $i$ is $\sum_s s m_{i,s}-v_i=0$. Its stabilizer is $$\mathcal W_{\lambda_m}
 =\mathcal W_m\times\prod_i S_{v_i-l_i}.$$ The last factors permute the zero slots.

By the degree convention in Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>, the half cohomological degree of $r_{\lambda_m}$ is the polynomial degree of its Laurent coefficient. For a remaining arrow $i\to j$, that degree is $$\sum_{a=1}^{v_i}\sum_{b=1}^{v_j}
       \max(s(ia)-s(jb),0)
 =v_iv_j-\sum_{a=1}^{v_i}\sum_{b=1}^{v_j}
                         \min(s(ia),s(jb)),$$ because $\sum_a s(ia)=v_i$. The framing contribution at $i$ is $w_i\sum_{d\in\Omega_m^+,\ i(d)=i}(s(d)-1)=w_i(v_i-l_i)$. Summing gives <a id="eq:multipartition-shift"></a>$$\label{eq:multipartition-shift}
 \delta_m:=\deg_q r_{\lambda_m}
 =C(\mathbf v)-D_m+\mathbf w\cdot(\mathbf v-\mathbf l).$$ This also proves integrality and nonnegativity. The formula includes remaining loops: their ordered pairs of gauge slots give precisely the diagonal term with $c_{ii}=2n_{ii}$.

For $-1\leq L\leq\sum_i v_i$, define $\mathcal F_{-1}=0$ and, for $L\geq0$, let $\mathcal F_L\subseteq B_0$ be the sum of the images of $$\mathsf S_{\lambda_m}\bigl(P^{\mathcal W_{\lambda_m}}\bigr),
 \qquad m\in\mathcal M(\mathbf v),\quad |l|\leq L.$$ Here and below an orbit sum denotes its image in the quotient when it is viewed in $B_0$. This is a finite filtration by graded vector subspaces. The array $m_{i,1}=v_i$ has $\lambda_m=0$, so the last stage contains the image of all coefficients. Those coefficients generate $B_0$, by Proposition <a href="#prop:minuscule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minuscule">3</a>; hence $\mathcal F_{\sum_i v_i}=B_0$. Exhaustion is asserted in the quotient, and requires no assertion about spanning the entire topological-degree-zero part of $A_0$ before quotienting. For later use denote the symbol map of each orbit type by $$\Phi_m:P^{\mathcal W_{\lambda_m}}
 \longrightarrow\mathcal F_{|l|}/\mathcal F_{|l|-1},\qquad
 p\longmapsto[\mathsf S_{\lambda_m}(p)].$$ It adds the half-degree shift $\delta_m$.

<a id="eq:zero-slot-power-sums"></a>
We first eliminate dressings in the zero slots. For each color $i$ and $k\geq1$, define an ambient Laurent-ring derivation by $$D_{i,k}(p e^\lambda)
 =p e^\lambda\sum_a\lambda_{ia}x_{ia}^k,
 \qquad D_{i,k}(p)=0\quad(p\in\mathbb C(x_{ia})).$$ Additivity of the displayed multiplier in $\lambda$ proves the Leibniz rule. This derivation commutes with $\mathcal W$, preserves the algebra in Equation <a href="#eq:laurent-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laurent-model">[eq:laurent-model]</a>, and preserves every topological homogeneous summand. It therefore preserves the ideal defining $B_0$. Its induced derivation on $B_0$ is zero, since it kills the coefficient generators. Applying it to an arbitrarily dressed orbit sum gives $$\label{eq:zero-slot-power-sums}
 \mathsf S_{\lambda_m}\left(
 p\left[
 \sum_{\substack{d\in\Omega_m^+\\i(d)=i}}
                   (s(d)-1)x_d^k
 -\sum_{\substack{z\in\Omega_m^0\\i(z)=i}}x_z^k
 \right]\right)=0\quad\text{in }B_0$$ for every $p\in P^{\mathcal W_{\lambda_m}}$. The invariant dressing ring is the tensor product of the symmetric-polynomial rings on the positive type blocks and the zero blocks. Power sums generate each zero-block ring over $\mathbb C$. Equation <a href="#eq:zero-slot-power-sums" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:zero-slot-power-sums">[eq:zero-slot-power-sums]</a>, including its arbitrary multiplier $p$, replaces each such power sum by the indicated positive-slot polynomial. Thus positive-slot dressings alone span the image of this orbit type. These replacements preserve degree.

Fix now a nonempty $S\subseteq\Omega_m^+$, and split the array of sizes on all gauge slots as $s=a+b$, by putting $b_d=s(d)$ on $S$ and $b_d=0$ elsewhere. Set $$\alpha=a-1,\qquad \beta=b.$$ The topological degree of $\beta$ is a nonzero vector with nonnegative entries, so every product $$\mathsf S_\alpha(p_a)\mathsf S_\beta(p_b)$$ vanishes in $B_0$. The dressings here may be arbitrary polynomials invariant under their respective coweight stabilizers, by Proposition <a href="#prop:laurent-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:laurent-model">12</a>.

To analyze the terms of this product, fix $a$ and match it with a colorwise permutation $b'$ of $b$. The resulting coweight is $t-1$, where $t=a+b'$ has nonnegative entries and $\sum_{i(d)=i}t_d=v_i$. It therefore determines another array in $\mathcal M(\mathbf v)$. Writing $A=\operatorname{supp}(a)$ and $B'=\operatorname{supp}(b')$, its length is $$|\operatorname{supp}(t)|
 =|A|+|B'|-|A\cap B'|=|l|-|A\cap B'|.$$ Every overlap term belongs to $\mathcal F_{|l|-1}$. For a disjoint matching, the positive sizes of $t$ are the multiset union of those of $a$ and $b$ at each color. Hence its array is exactly $m$; no other orbit type occurs at length $|l|$.

For the chosen disjoint decomposition, the excess coefficient in $r_\alpha r_\beta/r_{\lambda_m}$ is $q_S$ up to a nonzero constant sign. Here is the calculation for each weight. For an arrow from slot $d$ to slot $e$, its excess exponent is $$(a_d-a_e)_+ +(b_d-b_e)_+
                  -(a_d+b_d-a_e-b_e)_+.$$ This is zero unless one slot belongs to $\operatorname{supp}(a)$ and the other to $S$. In that case it is $\min(s(d),s(e))$. For the framing weight $-x_d$, the excess is $$(a_d-1)_+ +(b_d)_+ -(a_d+b_d-1)_+,$$ which equals one on $S$ and zero elsewhere. These give exactly the exponents in Equation <a href="#eq:cut-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-polynomial">[eq:cut-polynomial]</a>. With the weight conventions above, the sign is $(-1)^{\epsilon_S}$, where $$\epsilon_S=
 \sum_{d\in S}w_{i(d)}+
 \sum_{\substack{d\in S\\e\in\Omega_m^+\setminus S}}
 n_{i(d)i(e)}\min(s(d),s(e)).$$ This integer depends only on the numbers of selected and unselected copies of each type. In particular the sign is constant on the averaging orbit considered next.

<a id="eq:cut-leading-trace"></a>
It remains to check the full span of the leading dressing relations. Let $$H=\mathcal W_{\lambda_m},\qquad
 H_S=\mathcal W_\alpha\cap\mathcal W_\beta.$$ Then $H_S\subseteq H$. Its blocks are the selected and unselected copies of each positive type, together with the zero block at every color. All disjoint decompositions of the fixed representative $\lambda_m$ into translates of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ form exactly the set $H/H_S$. The coefficient of its leading orbit sum in the product above is $(-1)^{\epsilon_S}\operatorname{Tr}_{H_S}^H(q_Sp_ap_b)$. Since the sign is nonzero and all other terms have lower length, we obtain $$\label{eq:cut-leading-trace}
 \Phi_m\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}_{H_S}^H(q_Sp_ap_b)\bigr)=0,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Tr}_{H_S}^H(f)=\sum_{h\in H/H_S}h(f).$$

Choose $p_a$ to depend only on the positive-size blocks of $a$ and $p_b$ only on the positive-size blocks of $b$. Such choices are allowed by their coweight stabilizers. Their products span the $H_S$-invariant polynomial ring on the positive slots, since that ring is the tensor product of the symmetric-polynomial rings on the selected and unselected copies of every type. Moreover $q_S$ is $H_S$-invariant. For an arbitrary $f\in P_m$, Reynolds averaging first over $H_S$ and then over $H$ consequently shows that $$\operatorname{Av}_H(q_S f)
 =\operatorname{Av}_H\bigl(q_S\operatorname{Av}_{H_S}(f)\bigr)$$ is a linear combination of the relations in Equation <a href="#eq:cut-leading-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-leading-trace">[eq:cut-leading-trace]</a>. All normalizing factors are nonzero constants in $\mathbb C$. Averaging the generators $q_S$ with arbitrary multipliers spans $J_m\cap P_m^{\mathcal W_m}$, because $J_m$ is stable under $\mathcal W_m$ and averaging is exact in characteristic zero. Therefore all invariant cut-ideal relations annihilate the symbol of this orbit type.

Combining the zero-slot elimination with these cut relations gives, for every $L$, a surjection of graded vector spaces <a id="eq:filtration-symbol-surjection"></a>$$\label{eq:filtration-symbol-surjection}
 \bigoplus_{\substack{m\in\mathcal M(\mathbf v)\\|l|=L}}
 (P_m/J_m)^{\mathcal W_m}[\delta_m]
 \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal F_L/\mathcal F_{L-1}.$$ Here $[\delta_m]$ means that polynomial half degree $d$ is placed in half cohomological degree $d+\delta_m$. Exactness of invariants identifies the source with the invariant dressing quotient just obtained. Summing its Hilbert-series bound over the finite exhaustive filtration proves Equation <a href="#eq:filtration-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-bound">[eq:filtration-bound]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-rem-filtration-edge-cases"></a>
<div id="rem:filtration-edge-cases" class="remark">

**Remark 14**. If there are no gauge slots, the unique empty array gives $A_0=B_0=\mathbb C$ and equality in Equation <a href="#eq:filtration-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-bound">[eq:filtration-bound]</a>. If $\mathbf v\ne0$ and $\mathbf w=0$, the cut on all positive slots is $q_{\Omega_m^+}=1$, so every summand on the right is zero. Correspondingly, $e^{\mathbf 1}$ is an invertible monopole of nonzero topological degree, and $B_0=0$. The same observation applies to any unframed connected component. Full subsets, empty zero blocks, and rank-one gauge factors require no change in the proof.

</div>

The filtration has reduced the upper bound for $B_0$ to finitely many cut quotients $(P_m/J_m)^{\mathcal W_m}$. Section <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">6</a> evaluates their generating series and compares the sum in Equation <a href="#eq:filtration-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-bound">[eq:filtration-bound]</a> with the cohomology at determinant stability. No algebra presentation of $B_0$ is inferred from the vector-space surjections onto its filtration quotients.

<a id="sec:counting"></a>
### Cut ideals and the Betti-number bound

We retain the notation of Section <a href="#sec:laurent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:laurent">5</a>, including the assumption that the original quiver has a loop at every vertex of positive dimension. All Hilbert series in this section use half cohomological degree: each variable $x_d$ has degree one. We first compute the cut quotients and then identify the right-hand side of Proposition <a href="#prop:filtration-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtration-bound">13</a> with the ordinary Poincaré polynomial. We write $v=\mathbf v$, $w=\mathbf w$, and $l=(l_i)_i$ for the dimension vectors in this section.

For the counting identities, allow $m=(m_{i,s})_{i\in Q_0,\ s\geq1}$ to be any finite array of nonnegative integers. Its slots, cut ideal, and permutation group are defined as before. Set $$\|m\|_i=\sum_{s\geq1}s m_{i,s},\qquad
 w(m)=\sum_{i,s}w_i m_{i,s},\qquad
 (q)_m=\prod_{i,s}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{i,s}}(1-q^j).$$ For two such arrays, write $$b(k,m')=\sum_{i,j,s,t}c_{ij}\min(s,t)k_{i,s}m'_{j,t}.$$ Thus $D_m=\tfrac12 b(m,m)$. Empty products equal one, and the empty array has $P_0=\mathbb C$, $J_0=0$, and $h_0(q)=1$.

<a id="stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient"></a>
<div id="lem:finite-cut-quotient" class="lemma">

**Lemma 15**. *For every finite array $m$, the algebra $P_m/J_m$ is finite dimensional. Consequently $h_m(q)$ is a polynomial with nonnegative integer coefficients.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-finite-cut-quotient</summary>

*Proof.* The assertion for the empty array is immediate. Suppose that $x$ is a common zero of the cut polynomials and that one of its coordinates has a nonzero value $a$. Take $$S=\{d:x_d=a\}.$$ Every framing factor in $q_S(x)$ is nonzero, and every difference across the cut is nonzero because $S$ contains all slots with value $a$. Thus $q_S(x)\ne0$, a contradiction. The common zero set of $J_m$ is therefore contained in the origin. By the Nullstellensatz, either $J_m=P_m$ or a power of each variable belongs to $J_m$. Both alternatives give a finite-dimensional quotient. Taking invariants under the finite group $\mathcal W_m$ preserves finite dimensionality and the nonnegative grading. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-cut-recurrence"></a>
<div id="prop:cut-recurrence" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:cut-recurrence"></a>
**Proposition 16**. *For every finite array $m$, $$\label{eq:cut-recurrence}
 \frac{1}{(q)_m}
 =\sum_{k+m'=m}
   q^{w(k)+b(k,m')}\frac{h_{m'}(q)}{(q)_k}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-cut-recurrence"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-cut-recurrence</summary>

*Proof.* We realize the cut products as Euler classes of incoming coordinates and stratify by unreachable slots to obtain both the coefficient-ring presentation and the recurrence. Make a directed graph whose vertices are the slots of $m$. For each unordered pair of distinct slots $d,e$, put $b_{de}$ arrows from $d$ to $e$ and $b_{de}$ arrows from $e$ to $d$, with each ordered arrow counted once. Add a fixed framing vertex and $w_{i(d)}$ arrows from it to $d$. Assign a one-dimensional vector space to every slot and to the framing vertex. Let $E_m$ be the space of representations of this graph, and let $$T_m=(\mathbb C^\times)^{\Omega_m^+}$$ act by change of basis at the slots, leaving the framing line fixed. The class $x_d\in H_{T_m}^2(\mathrm{pt})$ is the first Chern class of the standard character at slot $d$.

<a id="eq:cut-cohomology"></a>
A slot is *reachable* if there is a directed path from the framing vertex to it on which every arrow coordinate is nonzero. Let $U_m$ be the open subset where all slots are reachable. We will show that the natural coefficient map gives $$\label{eq:cut-cohomology}
 H_{T_m}^*(U_m;\mathbb C)=P_m/J_m,$$ and that this cohomology is concentrated in even degrees. Reachability is an open condition: it is a union of conditions requiring the coordinates on finitely many paths to be nonzero.

We use induction on the number $n$ of slots. For $n=0$, the representation space and its reachable open are both a point, and both assertions hold. For $0\leq r\leq n$, let $U_r\subset E_m$ be the open subset with at most $r$ unreachable slots. Thus $U_n=E_m$ and $U_0=U_m$. For a subset $S$ of $r\geq1$ slots, let $Z_S\subset U_r$ consist of representations whose unreachable set is exactly $S$.

Let $L_S\subset E_m$ be the linear subspace on which all framing arrows into $S$ and all arrows from the complement of $S$ into $S$ vanish. These equations force every slot of $S$ to be unreachable. Conversely, within $U_r$, unreachability of all $r$ slots in $S$ forces every complementary slot to be reachable, so these incoming coordinates must vanish. It follows that $$Z_S=L_S\cap U_r.$$ In particular, $Z_S$ is smooth and closed in $U_r$, and $U_r\setminus U_{r-1}$ is the disjoint union of the $Z_S$ with $|S|=r$.

<a id="eq:cut-stratum-cohomology"></a>
Let $k$ record the types of the slots in $S$, and put $m'=m-k$. Restricting to the complement identifies $Z_S$ with a vector bundle over $U_{m'}$. Its remaining coordinates are the internal arrows on $S$ and the arrows from $S$ to its complement, all of which are unrestricted. Contracting these vector-bundle fibers is equivariant. The factor of $T_m$ acting at the slots of $S$ acts trivially on the base, so the inductive hypothesis gives $$\label{eq:cut-stratum-cohomology}
 H_{T_m}^*(Z_S)
 =H_{T_{m'}}^*(U_{m'})[x_d:d\in S]
 =(P_{m'}/J_{m'})[x_d:d\in S].$$

The normal bundle is the representation formed by the incoming coordinates set to zero. Its complex rank and equivariant Euler class are <a id="eq:cut-stratum-codimension"></a><a id="eq:cut-stratum-euler"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 c_S&=w(k)+b(k,m'),\label{eq:cut-stratum-codimension}\\
 e_S&=\prod_{d\in S}x_d^{w_{i(d)}}
       \prod_{d\in S,\ e\notin S}(x_d-x_e)^{b_{de}}
       =q_S.\label{eq:cut-stratum-euler}
\end{aligned}$$ Only arrows entering $S$ vanish; this accounts for the exponent $b(k,m')$, even though the graph has arrows in both directions.

Multiplication by $e_S$ on Equation <a href="#eq:cut-stratum-cohomology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-stratum-cohomology">[eq:cut-stratum-cohomology]</a> is injective. Indeed, view it as a polynomial in the variables indexed by $S$, over the possibly nonreduced coefficient ring $P_{m'}/J_{m'}$. Its term of highest total degree in those variables is the single monomial $$\prod_{d\in S}x_d^{w_{i(d)}+\sum_{e\notin S}b_{de}}$$ with coefficient one. Multiplication by this monomial is injective over any coefficient ring, and all other terms have lower degree in the new variables. A highest-degree argument proves the claim, including the case $e_S=1$.

<a id="eq:cut-thom-sequence"></a>
Write $i_S:Z_S\hookrightarrow U_r$. The self-intersection identity says that $i_S^*i_{S,*}$ is multiplication by $e_S$. Restriction to a different, disjoint stratum kills the Gysin image. Therefore the direct sum of the Gysin maps is injective. The equivariant Thom exact sequence, applied successively from $U_n$ down to $U_0$, gives short exact sequences $$\label{eq:cut-thom-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow
 \bigoplus_{|S|=r}H_{T_m}^{*-2c_S}(Z_S)
 \xrightarrow{\ \sum i_{S,*}\ }H_{T_m}^*(U_r)
 \longrightarrow H_{T_m}^*(U_{r-1})\longrightarrow0.$$ It also proves at each step that the cohomology is even and that restriction from $P_m=H_{T_m}^*(E_m)$ is surjective. These statements start with $U_n=E_m$; evenness of the strata follows from the induction on $n$.

We also identify the kernel at each step. By Equation <a href="#eq:cut-stratum-cohomology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-stratum-cohomology">[eq:cut-stratum-cohomology]</a>, every class on $Z_S$ is the restriction of a polynomial in the slot variables. The incoming linear equations cut out $Z_S$ regularly inside $U_r$, so its fundamental class $i_{S,*}(1)$ is the restriction of the Euler product $q_S$. The projection formula now shows that the image of $i_{S,*}$ is the ideal generated by that class. Removing all strata therefore gives exactly the ideal generated by the nonempty cuts, proving Equation <a href="#eq:cut-cohomology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-cohomology">[eq:cut-cohomology]</a>.

Finally, choose arrow labels compatibly with permutations of equal types. Then $\mathcal W_m$ acts on the entire construction, permuting the slot torus and its variables. Complex orientations give equivariant Thom classes without permutation signs. Taking $\mathcal W_m$-invariants is exact over $\mathbb C$. Subsets $S$ with a fixed count vector $k$ form one orbit, whose stabilizer is $\mathcal W_k\times\mathcal W_{m'}$. Their total invariant cohomology contribution in Equation <a href="#eq:cut-thom-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-thom-sequence">[eq:cut-thom-sequence]</a> is consequently $$q^{c_S}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}\bigl(\mathbb C[x_d:d\in S]^{\mathcal W_k};q\bigr)
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}\bigl((P_{m'}/J_{m'})^{\mathcal W_{m'}};q\bigr)
 =q^{w(k)+b(k,m')}\frac{h_{m'}(q)}{(q)_k}.$$ There is one such contribution for each count vector $k$; taking invariants of the sum over its subset orbit supplies no binomial multiplicity. The all-reachable open supplies the term $k=0$. Summing all strata and using $\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}(P_m^{\mathcal W_m};q)=1/(q)_m$ proves Equation <a href="#eq:cut-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-recurrence">[eq:cut-recurrence]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio"></a>
<div id="cor:cut-generating-ratio" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:cut-generating-ratio"></a>
**Corollary 17**. *In the formal power series ring $\mathbb C(q)[[z_i:i\in Q_0]]$, $$\label{eq:cut-generating-ratio}
 \sum_m z^{\|m\|}q^{-D_m-w(m)}h_m(q)
 =
 \frac{\displaystyle\sum_m
       z^{\|m\|}q^{-D_m-w(m)}/(q)_m}
      {\displaystyle\sum_m z^{\|m\|}q^{-D_m}/(q)_m}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cut-generating-ratio</summary>

*Proof.* The quadratic form satisfies $$D_{k+m'}=D_k+D_{m'}+b(k,m').$$ This identity includes the diagonal entries of the type matrix: slots in the two separate blocks are distinct even when their types agree. Multiply Equation <a href="#eq:cut-recurrence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-recurrence">[eq:cut-recurrence]</a> by $z^{\|m\|}q^{-D_m-w(m)}$ and sum over $m$. Its summand factors as $$\frac{z^{\|k\|}q^{-D_k}}{(q)_k}\,
 z^{\|m'\|}q^{-D_{m'}-w(m')}h_{m'}(q).$$ For each monomial in the $z_i$, only finitely many arrays occur.
